Ally McCoist singles out Liverpool star for blame after dramatic Fenerbahçe defeat

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

TNT Sports pundit Ally McCoist has criticised Liverpool goalkeeper Alisson Becker following the Reds’ dramatic 3-2 Champions League defeat against Fenerbahçe on Wednesday night.

Liverpool twice took the lead in Istanbul but failed to hold on, with substitute Vedat Muriqi scoring an 80th-minute winner to complete a remarkable comeback for the Turkish side.

McCoist was particularly critical of Alisson’s positioning for the decisive goal, questioning why the Brazilian goalkeeper failed to come off his line and claim the cross.

Speaking during TNT Sports’ live coverage, McCoist expressed his disbelief as Muriqi headed home Fenerbahçe’s third goal.

The former Scotland striker felt Alisson should have taken responsibility for dealing with the delivery rather than allowing the opposition striker to attack the ball.

“Brilliant header. When the ball comes in, I just think it’s in the air for a very long time, and I’m looking at my goalkeeper and I’m thinking where is Alisson?! The goalkeeper has to come for that.”

Liverpool made an encouraging start in Istanbul, with teenage midfielder Trey Nyoni opening the scoring in the 25th minute.

The 19-year-old produced a superb first-time volley from the edge of the penalty area to register his first senior goal for the club, becoming the second youngest goalscorer in the compeition for the club.

However, the Reds’ advantage lasted only four minutes.

Former Manchester United forward Mason Greenwood equalised for Fenerbahçe with a deflected free-kick that wrong-footed Alisson.

Liverpool regained control in the second half when substitute Alexander Isak found the net in the 69th minute to restore their lead.

Once again, though, Iraola’s side were unable to protect their advantage.

Archie Brown brought Fenerbahçe level for a second time, tapping home from close range following defensive confusion involving Alisson and Cody Gakpo.

The equaliser set up a tense finale, with Liverpool pushing to regain the lead while leaving themselves vulnerable at the back.

Fenerbahçe eventually capitalised in the 80th minute when Muriqi headed home the decisive goal.

The result completed a memorable comeback for former Liverpool forward Dirk Kuyt, who is now in charge of the Turkish club.

Julian Ward confirms decision on 'wonderful' Liverpool sensation

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Liverpool still have a lot of changes that need to be made to their first-team squad.

Even though over £600 million has been spent across the last couple of seasons, it still doesn't really feel like the Reds are any better off.

These are the thoughts of a lot of people because, with the January transfer window now just around the corner, fans are wanting even more additions to be made.

And, with how poor the Reds are in some areas of the pitch, these changes are well needed across the board.

However, before anything gets underway, some decisions on their current crop of players need to be made with Julian Ward now making his stance on one first-team star clear.

Liverpool are talking to Trey Nyoni about a contract extension

Trey Nyoni has been one of the breakout players among Liverpool's ranks this season.

The midfielder looked likely to be sent out on loan when it was Arne Slot at the helm but, from the very minute that Andoni Iraola took over, he wanted the Englishman to stay put.

Indeed, this was probably due to the fact that Curtis Jones was sold to Inter Milan, but, even so, he's now become an important first-team member.

In fact, he's arguably been the Reds' second-best central midfielder after Alexis Mac Allister.

This has led to fans wanting him to feature in every game possible with most of them rather taken aback after Iraola subbed him off against Arsenal on Sunday.

However, his performances haven't only just caught the eyes of fans as, according to The Athletic, Liverpool are now in "advanced talks" with the 19-year-old about a contract extension.

"Trey Nyoni is in advanced talks with Liverpool over a contract extension," they wrote.

"The 19-year-old has been discussing his future with the club for months and there’s hope a breakthrough will follow soon. While a new deal has yet to be finalised, there is optimism an agreement will be reached.

"Liverpool are keen to reward the young midfielder with fresh terms as he continues to impress under Andoni Iraola."

Alexis Mac Allister's time with Liverpool is clearly over

Liverpool managing to lock Nyoni down to a new long-term deal is obviously massive news.

However, it does feel like this could be the final nail in the coffin for Mac Allister's time as a Liverpool player.

As mentioned, the Argentinian has been the Reds' best midfielder this season but, with his contract set to expire in June 2028, the Reds are questioning whether it's worth offering him a new deal or not.

On the face of things, you'd think that Liverpool would jump at the chance to keep him around but, after Dominik Szoboszlai and Ryan Gravenberch were given new deals last season, there probably isn't that much money left for another long-term deal for a regular starter.

And, especially now that Nyoni is being offered a new deal, it feels like Ward has chosen against extending his contract.

Obviously, with a new ownership team likely to enter the frame in the near future, Mac Allister's situation could completely change but, right now, the "wonderful" midfielder's time with Liverpool looks like it's reached its natural end.

Criteo Is A Survivor, But Expanding Beyond Retail Media Will Be Its Toughest Challenge Yet

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Criteo has weathered considerable storms in its day, including a painful moment in March 2020 when its market cap bottomed out below $500 million.

But for a month now, Criteo’s market cap has hovered between $700 million and $750 million. So, can it pull off yet another turnaround?

The numbers from the company’s earnings report on Wednesday are … not great.

Criteo’s revenue decreased 12% year over year to $416 million. Its profit shrank from $40 million a year ago to $6 million in Q3 2026.

“This has been a challenging year,” Criteo CEO Michael Komasinski began the company’s investor call. “Our performance is not yet where I want it to be. The strength of our capabilities and the progress we’re making give me confidence in our direction.”

For Criteo, that “progress” mostly comes in the form of monetizing new media channels and formats. Criteo introduced CTV support last year. It’s also pursuing more LLM chatbot integrations after being the first ad tech vendor partner for ChatGPT. And it’s striking social media ecommerce integrations with the likes of TikTok Shop and Meta.

Criteo underwent a similar evolution many years ago, when it shifted from a retargeting business to retail media, which was one of the high-growth, high-optimism channels at the time (and also now).

But during its earnings call, investors pressed on whether retail media was a mature channel. They wondered if retail media growth slowing down is a result of Criteo customers requiring fewer client services. Or maybe it speaks to a general dearth of new retail media business opportunities to tap.

There could be something to those concerns, since Criteo seems to have more room for growth in nonretail channels.

CTV, AI platform advertising and social media integrations now represent 13% of spend for Criteo Commerce Growth, the company’s AI-based optimization product. That’s up from 6% a year ago.

CTV, LLM and social ads are also outpacing Criteo’s legacy display ad web targeting business, which is down year over year.

Can Criteo turn those emerging channels into real revenue pillars for years to come? That proposition remains unproven.

On the LLM front, “ChatGPT has been our fastest-growing partnership and channel for some time,” Komasinski said. But “fastest growing” comes with caveats. Percentage growth can be through the roof even if the line item went from a smidge above zero to a number that’s still quite small. We can’t really say either way. Criteo did not provide a hard revenue number for its LLM business.

Still, one important proof point for ChatGPT Ads growth is its market spread, Komasinski said. Criteo was in seven countries with ChatGPT a few months ago. Now it’s in 89 countries.

Plus, ChatGPT Ads traffic spends 15% more per visit on average, he said, which could help secure bigger budgets from brands who’ve seen success in early tests.

Thus far, Criteo has had “3,600 clients on and off the platform,” Komasinski said.

He explained the specific and strange wording of “on and off the platform,” which he said is because brands are mostly not “always-on” advertisers with ChatGPT (and other AI platforms). Looking ahead to Q4, Komasinski expects brands to run test campaigns over the holidays and deconstruct the results. Then they’ll probably come back and test again. How long it’ll take for those budgets to grow and become sticky is anyone’s guess, but Komasinski said he expects to see revenue growth ramping next year.

Meanwhile, third-party ad tech can’t complain about getting access to a trendy new platform.

“We’ve got a great position with a fast-growing channel, fast-growing partner who is introducing functionality at a rapid pace,” Komasinski said of the ChatGPT Ads partnership.

Of the three new channels Criteo is prioritizing, social is the most mature. In the past year, social media spend on its platform and total social campaigns were up 20%.

But Criteo is still very early in working its way into CTV.

“It took us a little time to tune our bidder, build the right campaign structure (and) the right workflow” for streaming TV ads, Komasinski said.

Getting advertisers to approach TV as a performance or direct response-caliber media also requires a change in education and industry thinking, he said. But performance CTV is “a great fit for what we do,” he said, and the required mindset shift around CTV as a performance channel is “now coming to fruition.”

For Criteo, there are other reasons to pursue these new media channels, even if it means starting from zero with whole new types of ad formats and workflow software.

Apple’s crackdown on ID infrastructure is a great motivator for moving beyond display and retail media, which require extensive cross-site tracking.

“We’re not currently seeing an impact from the Safari changes in iOS 27, supported by our longstanding investments in privacy-safe technology and durable addressability,” Komasinski said multiple times when addressing questions about Apple’s recent changes to WebKit and iOS.

Of course, iOS 27 gained little adoption during Criteo’s Q3, one investor pointed out. So the effects naturally haven’t arrived yet, right?

Komasinski declined to weigh in any further on WebKit specifics.

However, he added, “our channel diversification with social, OpenAI and CTV just puts us in a fundamentally different position today than where Criteo was a couple of years ago.”

Disaffection and outright anger toward US sports institutions run deeper than logo-cluttered fields. Leagues and teams are sacrificing player safety and fan enjoyment for marketing dollars.

People Inc.’s referral traffic from Google search is down 70% over the past two years, and by 26% in Q3 alone. But its digital revenue is still growing, albeit slowly.

On Tuesday, Chord, a six-year-old commerce tech startup, acquired Zenlytic, an AI startup with an anthropomorphized data analyst agent named “Zoë,” for $5 million.

If you ask the executive leadership of Affle and Perion why the two companies are merging, the answer you’ll get is “synergies.”

A combo of Tubi, sports and political ads powered Fox’s growth this last quarter, even as Fox News lost viewers and the Roku deal awaits DOJ clearance.

It’s fall, and M&A is in the air. Two acquisitions happened this week: Stagwell bought Freestar, and DoorDash bought Instacart.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

The Prior Occupants of Westbeth Are Just Beyond Reach

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A little more legibility would've benefited Alison Nguyen's fragmented short film about the famed NYC artists' housing complex.

Near the start of the High Line in Manhattan, a short film screening to the public above 14th Street dances around the history of the Westbeth Artists Housing complex, a stone's throw away from the park. While clearly drawn from extensive research and intrigue, Alison Nguyen's 14-minute film “Prior Occupants” (2026) skates on vague fragments of information that do better to incite curiosity rather than illustrate outright.

Have you ever sat through a lecture while feverishly looking up the keywords being mentioned to appear less woefully out of the loop? Nguyen begins by paging through confidential files attributed to the American industrial development company Bell Laboratories, but does little to explain their significance beyond fleeting references to the company's involvement in the Manhattan Project and developing components for ballistic missiles.

These Signature Sony Headphones Are Under $200 Ahead of Black Friday

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Sony WH-1000XM6 headphones are the most recent entry in Sony's 1000X series, which has been around since 2016. They're the best over-ear headphones in terms of audio quality currently on the market—but not everyone can (or needs to) drop nearly half a grand on headphones.

The WH-1000XM5 are a generation older and were at the top of their class until they were supplanted by the XM6. On the plus side, by virtue of being older, they're currently discounted to $198 (down from the original $399.99) during Amazon's early Black Friday sale. That 50% off brings it down to the lowest price they've ever reached, according to price-tracking tools, a good deal for premium headphones that still sound incredible, even in 2026.

I have been a loyal customer of the 1000X line for many years; they're my go-to headphones for most activities. The WH-1000XM5 received an "outstanding" review from PCMag for their top-notch audio quality and exceptional audio when using the best-in-class active noise cancellation. The headphones are also well-designed to be comfortable for long listening sessions. The ear controls use tapping and swiping, which isn't my favorite, but it's what all modern headphones are moving toward. There's a companion app that lets you adjust your EQ settings to your liking, including what the swiping and tapping functions do.

These headphones also have a stereo 3.5mm connection, which is perfect for those who want to use a cable and not worry about battery life. And speaking of battery life, Sony says you can expect about 30 hours, but it will vary depending on your use of ANC. They are compatible with AAC, LDAC, and SBC codecs and have multipoint connection, meaning you can pair them with more than one device at the same time.

Personally, I think the value for the money you get with the XM5 for $198 is better than paying full price for the XM6. If you're looking to get ahead of the Black Friday chaos and secure an amazing deal on great headphones, this is it.

Everton are reportedly interested in signing Minnesota United striker Kelvin Yeboah during the January transfer window as David Moyes looks to strengthen his attacking options.

The Toffees have identified the Italian forward as a potential addition, with his impressive performances in Major League Soccer attracting attention from several European clubs.

According to a report from Football Insider, Everton are prepared to make a move for the 26-year-old, with sources describing a deal as realistic. The Merseyside club believe Yeboah could represent a relatively affordable option compared to other attacking targets in the European market.

Yeboah has been in excellent form for Minnesota United, scoring 18 goals and providing four assists in 32 MLS appearances this season. His goalscoring record has understandably attracted interest, and a return to European football could be an attractive opportunity for the striker.

The forward is under contract with Minnesota until December 2027, meaning the American club are in a position to demand a transfer fee. However, Everton hope to take advantage of the circumstances and negotiate a deal without overspending.

The Friedkin Group’s decision to put Everton up for sale has also created some uncertainty surrounding the club’s transfer budget. Although they have committed to funding operations until a sale is completed, the recruitment team may need to work within financial limits.

Meanwhile, Moyes is keen to bring in another striker in January, and Yeboah could provide useful competition and additional firepower in the final third.

His scoring numbers suggest that he has the qualities to make an impact, although adapting to the intensity and physical demands of the Premier League would represent a significant challenge.

Everton will need to assess their options carefully, but Yeboah could prove to be a sensible investment if they can agree on favourable financial terms with Minnesota.

2027 Lamborghini Temerario

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

See all results for new 2027 Lamborghini Temerario for sale near 85032

The Temerario might be the runt of the Lamborghini supercar litter, but it's still one raging bull. Taking the reins from its widely lauded Huracan predecessor, the Temerario is the least expensive way into Lambo's supercar lineup, but it doesn’t sacrifice much performance compared to the company's Revuelto flagship—in fact, it beats its brawnier older brother to 60 mph by a tenth of a second.

Under the Temerario's origami-folded sheetmetal lies a newly developed 4.0-liter twin-turbo V-8 nestled into the car's aluminum spaceframe just ahead of the rear axle. As if 789 horsepower from the engine alone wasn’t enough, a trio of electric motors—one mounted between the engine and transmission and one at each front wheel—ups its total output to 907 Italian stallions. If you somehow tire of the sound of the flat-plane-crank V-8's goosebump-raising 10,000-rpm wail, the electric motors also allow for short periods of silent, all-electric driving. With its mighty hybrid-assisted thrust, poster-worthy looks, and agile moves, the Temerario is every inch a purebred Lambo and well worthy of supercar status.

Expert Tip: If you'd rather have a horse instead of a bull in your garage, the Ferrari 296GTB is a steed with the need for speed, and moves to match the Lambo for a similar price.

Compare the 2027 Lamborghini Temerario with the top vehicles in this segment with our new compare tool.

Lamborghini's newest bull debuted in 2026 and charges into 2027 without any notable changes.

If this year's model is largely unchanged and you're comfortable with a lightly used car, you may want to consider shopping previous model years. A one-to-three-year-old model will likely have a warranty and it'll cost less than buying new.

The Temerario's powertrain consists of a twin-turbo 4.0-liter V-8 with a flat-plane crankshaft, a trio of axial-flux electric motors, and an eight-speed dual-clutch transmission. The Temerario's V-8 alone is good for 789 horsepower; adding the output of the three electric motors brings the total combined output to 907 horsepower. That's just 94 ponies shy of the Lamborghini Revuelto, the brand's V-12 halo car. On the Temerario, one electric motor mounts to the V-8 in front of the transmission, while the remaining two live up front, one at each wheel. The Temerario's V-8 redlines at 10,000 rpm, and the engine's peak torque of 538 pound-feet is available from 4000 to 7000 rpm. While the flat-plane-crank V-8 sounds glorious when pushed to its limit, we wish that its exhaust packed a bit more fury earlier in the rev range.

With the electric motors in play, maximum torque output rises to 590 pound-feet. In addition to supplying enormous power, the hybrid configuration helps provide instantaneous torque and linear power delivery over a broad range of speeds and driving situations. Thirteen drive programs are available via a rotary button on the steering wheel, including launch and drift modes, the latter offering three levels of tail-out leeway. We drove the Temerario at the track and on the street and were enthralled with its lively powertrain and playful demeanor.

Car and Driver calculates your car's value in three free and easy steps - using the same Black Book® data dealers use to appraise vehicles.

In Comparison: In our testing, the Temerario got to 60 mph in a ridiculously quick 2.1 seconds. It also ripped through the quarter-mile in 9.4 seconds at 150 miles per hour. Both figures beat its big sibling, the Revuelto, by 0.3 seconds and 2 mph, respectively.

Juice is stored in a 3.8-kWh lithium-ion battery pack. The battery resides in the center tunnel along with the transmission—the location is said to help optimize weight distribution and is protected from underneath by structural elements. Regenerative braking and power from the internal-combustion engine both replenish the battery's state of charge. Plug-in charging is also possible via a standard 7-kW onboard charger; with typical household current, it can recharge an empty battery to full capacity in a claimed 30 minutes. An EV-only mode provides around 4 miles of range.

The EPA estimates that the Temerario will deliver 14 mpg city, 19 mpg highway, and 16 mpg combined. When we get a chance to test the Temerario, we'll update this story with results from our 75-mph highway fuel economy test. For more information about the Temerario's fuel economy, visit the EPA's website.

First Row: Thanks to its all-aluminum chassis, the Temerario's interior offers more space than the Huracán it replaced. Headroom increased by 1.3 inches, legroom by 1.8 inches, and Lamborghini claims the interior is large enough to accommodate drivers up to six feet five wearing a helmet. The interior shares some styling cues from the Revuelto; four stitching patterns are available, and the 18-way adjustable driver's seat is heated and ventilated. The Temerario also borrows the Revuelto's steering wheel, which is too busy with controls for our liking.

Cargo Capacity: If you're planning on taking the Temerario on a weekend getaway, you'd better pack light; the front trunk only has a four-cubic-foot capacity.

Lamborghini says the Temerario offers the most advanced suite of infotainment and connectivity features of any model in its lineup. Three displays—a 12.3-inch instrument panel with three user-selectable views, an 8.4-inch infotainment screen, and a 9.1-inch passenger screen—occupy prime dashboard real estate. Wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto are standard, as is a Sonus Faber audio system.

The most notable feature, though, is the optional Lamborghini Vision Unit (LAVU) system. Essentially an on-board data acquisition system, the setup utilizes three cameras: one viewing the road, one focused on the passengers, and one looking forward over the driver's shoulder. Preloaded with data for 150 different racing circuits, the system can be used to evaluate and improve driver performance. It also enables a "memories recorder" function that allows for up to two minutes of off-track recording, and works as a dashcam that can capture one minute of incident history.

Compared to most other cars, the Temerario doesn't offer many driver-assistance features.

For information about the Temerario's crash-test results, visit the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) and Insurance Institute for Highway Safety (IIHS) websites.

Compared with most mainstream automakers, Lamborghini doesn't provide stellar warranty coverage. But its protection plans are competitive with those of high-end rivals such as Ferrari and can be enhanced by optional extended coverage. A five-year complimentary scheduled maintenance plan is now standard.

Each year, we put hundreds of vehicles through our rigorous instrumented testing regimen. If the data in the chart below is for a different model year, that's because this vehicle hasn't changed from when we last tested it, and the test results remain accurate.

Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Home » Crypto Market News » Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

As 131 million ADA tokens sit idle in retired pools, the weekly chart structure points to $1.37 as next-cycle destination.

Cardano is trading near $0.19 on a weekly chart that one analyst compares directly to the base that preceded a roughly 2,000% rally in the last cycle. At the same time, on-chain data shows 131.63 million ADA remains tied to retired stake pools, leaving tens of thousands of holders without rewards until they redelegate.

360Trader highlighted the weekly structure and noted that ADA remains well below the level where a true bull-market stage would begin. That stage, in the analyst’s view, only starts once price clears $1.37.

The more immediate requirement is a reclaim of $0.25. Until then the chart is still forming the same kind of low base that previously launched a 2,000% advance once the broader altcoin market turned. The comparison is structural rather than a near-term price target.

Chart Request winner: $ADA 📊Weekly chart, and it's still well under its bull-market stage. That stage really doesn't start until $1.37 breaks.First it needs to get back above $0.25 quickly before I'd be interested.Last cycle ADA went 2000% off a base just like this one.… pic.twitter.com/4BPAusTmTY

From current levels a move to the prior cycle high would still require a multi-hundred-percent gain, and the larger $1.37 threshold sits even further away.

ADA has not yet reclaimed $0.25, the level 360Trader said would be needed before the setup becomes interesting again. The token has been consolidating near the lower end of its multi-year range while the wider crypto market digests recent pressure.

The analyst’s point is that the base itself is not the signal. Confirmation only arrives when price begins to push out of the accumulation zone and the broader altcoin cycle starts to expand.

Until those conditions appear, the historical 2,000% precedent remains a reminder of what has happened from comparable structures rather than a forecast for the next few weeks.

In related Cardano news, Cexplorer data flagged a practical on-chain issue. Roughly 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to 2,720 retired stake pools, totaling 131.63 million ADA.

Those pools no longer produce blocks or distribute rewards. Holders retain full control of their tokens and can move the delegation to an active pool at any time. The funds are not locked or lost, but every epoch spent on a retired pool is simply a missed reward.

Cardano Has a 131M ADA Staking ProblemThousands of Cardano (@Cardano) holders may need to check where their $ADA is delegated.Cexplorer reports that 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to retired stake pools. The total comes to 131.63 million ADA.The explorer… pic.twitter.com/cdTn46aFLr

Community responses noted that checking a pool’s status on an explorer and redelegating takes only a minute and restores eligibility for the next epoch’s rewards.

The technical observation and the staking data sit side by side. One points to a historical base that once produced a 2,000% move once conditions aligned.

The other shows that a measurable portion of the staked supply is currently idle because pool operators retired and holders have not yet updated their delegations.

ADA remains near $0.19, below the $0.25 reclaim level and far from the $1.37 threshold that would mark a shift into a broader bull-market phase.

Whether the base develops the same way as last cycle depends on price reclaiming those levels and on the wider altcoin market providing the liquidity. In the meantime, any Cardano holder still delegated to a retired pool can restore rewards by simply choosing an active one.

Pangram expands its AI detection tools to video and images

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The company is moving into more kinds of AI slop.

Pangram, maker of a widely used tool for detecting AI-generated text, released new tools for flagging AI-generated image and video slop.

The company says that the underlying model behind its video detection capabilities "particularly excels at frontier AI video detection" and that in testing it was significantly better at picking out AI videos than other detection tools. The video model was able to flag AI content with more than 98 percent accuracy, according to one internal test detailed by the company.

Pangram has also upgraded its AI image detection abilities. The company previously released a research preview of its image model, Pangram Image 1.0. That model is now available via the company's Chrome extension, which can automatically scan images as you browse and flag ones that are likely to be AI.

According to the company, its image model was able to flag more than 99 percent of AI images with a false positive rate of .27 percent in its testing. Of course, as with other AI image detection tools, there are some caveats. The company says that at resolutions below 512 x 512, "results will come with a reduced confidence disclaimer." That could account for why an image shared by Pangram CEO Max Spero of the Chrome extension in action on Pinterest's website indicated an "unsure" result on two images that are obviously not genuine photos.

Spero noted that there were other challenges to developing its image detection model. "Most phone cameras, including iPhones, do some level of AI postprocessing to add detail to real photos," he explained in a post on X. "We had to build a model that's robust to this postprocessing while understanding the difference between AI edits and fully AI-generated visuals." And he told Pirate Wires that the company delayed the release of its image model so it could test it on several iPhone 18 Pros because its model initially flagged images from the phones' cameras as AI.

Pangram's new AI image and video detection abilities are available now to those with a Pangram subscription. The company allows users with a free account to check a handful of images without signing up for a paid plan.

France Opens War Crimes Investigation Into Israeli Airstrike That Killed Artist Ali Cherri's Parents

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Amnesty International found that Israeli forces had no evidence of a verifiable military target at the site of the strike.

France's War Crimes Unit has opened an investigation into a deadly Israeli airstrike on French-Lebanese artist and filmmaker Ali Cherri's family home in Barbour, Beirut. The investigation emerges seven months after the artist filed a complaint accusing Israel of war crimes with the office, as the 2024 strike on the residential building killed both of his parents, the domestic worker they employed in the apartment Cherri owned, and at least four other civilians living in the building.

In a February report on Israeli war crimes in Lebanon, Amnesty International stated that Israeli forces had no evidence of a verifiable military target at the site of the strike, and concluded that Israeli forces failed to issue an evacuation warning to civilians before conducting the strike on the 12-story apartment building on November 26, 2024 — less than 12 hours before the temporary ceasefire between Hezbollah and Israel came into effect. Constructed by the Cherri family, the building suffered structural damage localized to the eastern apartments on the 7th to 10th floors. Cherri's late parents, Mahmoud Naim Cherri and Nadira Hayek, as well as domestic worker Birki Negesa, were found in the wreck of the artist's ninth-floor apartment where they resided.

Cherri and his legal representatives at the International Federation for Human Rights (FIDH) also commissioned the London-based investigative research agency Forensic Architecture to digitally reconstruct the apartment building based on the artist's and neighboring witnesses' documentation of the site post-strike. Both Amnesty International's and Forensic Architecture's findings informed Cherri's complaint accusing Israel of war crimes by “intentionally directing an attack against a civilian object.”

The Luxury Fashion Police Are Coming for the Makeup-Bags-as-a-Purse Girls. But They Should Back Off

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Anything can be a bag if you really want it to be and the internet outrage around this says more about elitism than style.

There is a particular breed of luxury fashion enthusiast who is personally offended by the idea of a woman taking an expensive bag that is not technically designed to be a purse and, well, using it as a purse. The latest offenders? Anyone turning Hermès Bride-a-Brac pouches and Bolide cosmetic cases, or Chanel makeup bags into their go-to everyday handbag. To these particular fashion enthusiasts, doing such a thing is basically committing a fashion felony.

The argument is that these products are not real handbags. That wearing a cosmetic case on your shoulder is “tacky” and that it cheapens the brand as a whole (presumably for those who can afford the usually even pricier actual bags). But why does it matter?

The Hermès Bride-a-Brac, for example, was originally designed as a wash bag or travel pouch. It has a structured canvas body, a wide-opening zippered top, and it is perfectly suited to hold all the things women actually carry around with them: A phone, cardholder, sunglasses, keys, lip products, and whatever other miscellaneous objects that accumulate in the bottom of a purse.

There is also something particularly funny about the outrage of women repurposing these bags when you consider the history of luxury handbags themselves. The Hermès Evelyne began as a grooming bag for horse equipment, designed with perforations to allow brushes to dry. The Louis Vuitton Noé was originally created to carry bottles of champagne. These are now some of the most recognizable luxury bags in the world.

Even the mythology surrounding the Birkin has always been rooted in the idea of a bag that is meant to be lived in. Jane Birkin largely saw the staple piece as an everyday work bag meant to be heavily used and filled to the brim. The original vision was not necessarily some precious object to be locked away in a dust bag and admired from across the bedroom. So it actually makes perfect sense for people to use their makeup bags as purses, if they so please.

This idea that people shouldn’t do this because it’s “tacky” quickly reveals the class politics hiding underneath so much of the fashion industry. Luxury has become wrapped up in exclusivity that people have started treating the products themselves as elitist identifiers. Owning the right bag, wearing the right logo, and knowing the unwritten rules of fashion are all supposedly proof that you belong to a certain world. And when someone finds a creative way to participate without following the exact spending requirements, it seems to make certain people very uncomfortable.

I will never forget sitting at a large dinner table when another fashion editor asked me when I was finally going to invest in a “real designer bag,” suggesting that my $400 Coach purse simply wasn't good enough. I remember asking her, quite sincerely, whether she planned on buying it for me if it was such a pressing concern. She didn’t have an answer, but I’ll never forget how it made me feel. That fashion and personal style has a price tag we all aren’t meant to meet.

You can love Hermès and still think a Bride-a-Brac makes a great everyday bag. You can appreciate Chanel's history and still think a cosmetic pouch looks adorable with a chain strap. These ideas are not mutually exclusive.

Women have been repurposing pieces in their closets forever. We have quite literally always done things like turn belts into necklaces, oversized shirts into dresses, and whatever else happens to be lying around into something worth wearing. Fashion and improvisation go hand in hand. Some of the most interesting personal style I’ve seen comes from people who aren't particularly concerned with whether an item was originally intended to be worn a certain way. A bag is a bag if it holds your things and you like carrying it. People are making this much more complicated than it needs to be.

Moreover, I also don't believe in pocket-watching. Not everyone has thousands of dollars to spend on a handbag, and they shouldn't feel obligated to. If someone's luxury budget allows for a Chanel makeup bag rather than a classic flap bag, and they decide to put a strap on it and carry it to dinner, where exactly is the problem? If anything, I find it entertaining to see someone get creative with what they already own.

At the end of the day, there are so many bigger things going on in the world. None of this is that serious. A makeup bag can be a purse. A scarf can be a belt. A skirt can be a top. And a woman does not need the approval of someone else to wear what she wants how she wants to wear it.

Aiyana Ishmael is the style editor at Cosmopolitan magazine. In her work, Aiyana focuses on the culture of fashion and how it intertwines and shapes the zeitgeist. She is an award-winning journalist from Miami, Florida, and a graduate of the historically Black university, Florida A&M. She is a 2024 Forbes 30 Under 30: Media honoree, and her debut romance novella PASSING GAME is set to release March of 2027 (831 Stories/Simon & Schuster).

‘Blade Runner 2099’ Shows the ‘Silver Linings’ of a Replicant Takeover

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ridley Scott’s 1982 film Blade Runner casts a long shadow. In some ways, literally.

It defined what noir sci-fi would look like for years to come: the rain, the dramatic lighting, the trenchcoats. Denis Villeneuve delivered a worthy sequel in 2017 with Blade Runner 2049, but for some fans nothing will ever touch the original.

Silka Luisa tries not to think about these things. “If I didn’t love Blade Runner this would’ve been way too hard,” the showrunner of Prime Video’s limited series Blade Runner 2099 said Wednesday during the WIRED World Fair event in Miami.

Luisa’s show differs quite a bit from its forebears. It still has the humanoids known as replicants, but in 2099’s future, they’re the ones calling the shots, giving humans the grunt work they used to do. Set 80 years after Blade Runner and 50 years after 2049, it focuses on a replicant Blade Runner, Olwen (Michelle Yeoh), on the search for rogue agents. Along the way she gets teamed up with Cora, played by Hunter Schafer, a human pretending to be a replicant to survive. (We’d say more, but the show doesn’t premiere until November 25, and, you know, spoilers.)

If this vision of dystopia sounds too familiar for comfort, Luisa hears you. When asked about how she chose to tackle the franchise, she noted that in the last couple of decades, “the present caught up to the films” as megacorporations and environmental collapse became realities in ways they weren’t in 1982. The point of her Blade Runner is to show what happens in a post-revolution world and “not focus on the fears we’re living with right now.”

That doesn’t mean that Blade Runner 2099 is entirely a dystopian tale. For Luisa, it’s about showing the “silver linings” of a world run by replicants. To do that, she picks up on some of the environmental themes touched on in Villeneuve’s movie, which showed how Earth had been wrecked by human activity. Blade Runner 2099 depicts a world that replicants are trying to bring back from the brink of ecological collapse using the skills they gained when they were working for humans. “They’re able to rehabilitate the planet,” Luisa says.

Blade Runner 2099 also gave Schafer a chance to flex some action-hero muscles—something she’d never done before and something her costar, Yeoh, has been doing for decades. Schafer jumped right in, running and diving across the screen from the pilot onward. For the fight scenes, though, she got some help from Yeoh.

“It was really physically demanding,” Schafer said during WIRED’s conference. “I felt so lucky to have Michelle Yeoh, who is the fucking master, as a scene partner.”

But back to that shadow cast by Scott’s original adaptation of Philip K. Dick’s novel Do Androids Dream of Electric Sheep? In addition to being a sci-fi classic, it also created a whole new template for what dystopian fiction could look like. It broods.

Luisa insists there’s a little more hope in hers—and hopes audiences see that, too. She also wants audiences to know that replicants aren’t the same as the AI bots humans are fixated on in 2026. “They’re more akin to us,” she said. “AI, I just don’t have that same sympathy for.” Hear that, Claude?

Amodei Wins Dismissal of Direct Copyright Claim with Leave to Amend

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Judge Eumi K. Lee of the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on November 4, 2026 dismissed the direct copyright infringement claim against Anthropic co-founder Dario Amodei, with leave to amend, according to the court’s November 4, 2026 minute entry.

The minute entry left Anthropic’s separate partial motion unresolved. Lee ordered the parties to meet and confer on whether to address that motion through additional briefing or by amending the complaint and to file a joint status report by November 11, 2026. The case, Concord Music Group, Inc. v. Anthropic PBC, names Anthropic, Amodei and Benjamin Mann as defendants.

Amodei’s August 3, 2026 motion targeted only the claim that he directly infringed copyrights through torrenting, not the separate contributory-infringement claim. He argued that the complaint attributes the downloads to Mann and other Anthropic employees, while allegations that Amodei directed, approved, knew of or encouraged that conduct describe contributory liability rather than unlawful copying by him personally.

Under that argument, direct infringement requires unlawful copying by the defendant, while contributory infringement addresses knowingly inducing, causing or materially contributing to another person’s infringement. Torrenting involves breaking files into pieces and redistributing those pieces among users. Amodei sought dismissal with prejudice, but leave to amend instead gives the publishers an opportunity to revise the direct-infringement allegation. His motion did not put the contributory theory at issue.

Anthropic’s August 3, 2026 motion sought dismissal of the portion of the direct-infringement claim based on Claude’s outputs and the entire Digital Millennium Copyright Act claim concerning copyright-management information, or CMI. CMI includes identifying information conveyed with a work, such as its title, songwriter and copyright owner. Anthropic did not move to dismiss the separate direct-infringement claim based on torrenting or the portion of the training-and-output claim concerned with training, although it contests those claims’ merits.

Anthropic argued that the complaint supplies no specific example of an infringing Claude output and does not distinguish which works were allegedly copied in training from those allegedly reproduced in outputs. It also argued that the CMI claim fails because the allegations concern lyrics rather than complete copyrighted musical compositions, and requested dismissal with prejudice. The dispute therefore concerns whether the publishers pleaded their output and CMI theories with enough specificity, not whether any particular Claude response has been proven to copy a song.

The second amended complaint filed May 21, 2026 identifies 20,517 compositions and alleges that Anthropic copied the publishers’ lyrics without permission for training, generated verbatim and near-verbatim copies in Claude outputs, and used guardrails that covered only a limited set of songs and did not comprehensively prevent copying from the broader catalog at issue. The publishers’ theory links those stages: the initial copying allegedly encoded the lyrics in Claude, and further training and fine-tuning allegedly produced copies in response to user requests.

In their August 31, 2026 opposition, the publishers argued that they need not identify specific infringing-output examples at the pleading stage and that records of third-party users’ prompts and Claude’s corresponding outputs are in Anthropic’s possession and control. They asked the court to deny Anthropic’s motion in full or, alternatively, permit another amendment.

On CMI, the publishers argued that scanning and torrenting songbooks and sheet music copied complete musical compositions and that extraction algorithms removed identifying information during training. That response addresses Anthropic’s distinction between lyrics and complete works, which is central to the unresolved part of the company’s motion.

The practical effect is narrower than a ruling on Claude itself. The publishers can attempt to repair the direct-copying allegation against Amodei, while his motion did not challenge their contributory theory. The separate dispute over Claude’s outputs and ownership information remains open, with amendment and further briefing now the two paths under discussion.

If you’ve been waiting for a deal on an iconic premium fitness tracker that does it all, the Apple Watch Ultra 4 is worth checking out. The new Ultra 4 is designed for those who want much more than a basic activity tracker. It combines health and fitness features with a large display, cellular connectivity, GPS, and a battery that can keep going for more than two days between charges, depending on how you use it.

In her comparison of the Ultra 4 and Series 12 for Women’s Health, associate health and fitness editor Addison Aloian found that the larger screen made it easier to read texts and check workout stats while running or lifting. Avoid California’s Crowded Trails With These 5 Quiet Coastline Hikes

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There’s no shortage of spectacular hiking in California. Big Sur, Yosemite, and Redwoods have achieved international renown––and for good reason. But if you want to escape the crowds, the less congested San Luis Obispo coast offers scores of trails, uncrowded wilderness, and stellar vistas along Highway 1.

These routes invite you to hike on hills turned gold by spring flowers, spot seals in coastal reserves, and explore both long and short routes near cozy coastal towns—all without fighting for a parking spot at the trailhead. Integrate these hikes into a road trip along the iconic Highway 1, or just visit for an active getaway. San Luis Obispo’s landscapes are beautiful, the food is delicious, and, for the nights you’re not camping, there’s great lodging.

Named for the abundant yellow wildflowers that paint the hills gold every spring—namely bright yellow poppies, Deerbrush, and wild mustard—this unique park along the Morro Bay estuary is home to riparian environments, coastal scrub, and pines.

Scores of trails weave across 8,000 acres of wilderness and offer views of Morro Bay. But for epic views of the coast and a solid taste of all the park has to offer, start at the Hazard Peak Trailhead on Pecho Valley Road and hike out on Hazard Trail to the peak. From there, you can retrace your steps back to the junction with Canyon View Trail. Take that and descend a series of short switchbacks to Islay Creek Road, which will spit you out just down the street from the Hazard Peak Trailhead. You can also extend the hike by doing the full Hazard Peak loop, which is about 11 miles.

Located in the Lopez Lake Recreation Area outside Arroyo Grande, this trail climbs up and along the western ridge of the county park. Wildlife sightings of deer, birds, even black bears, aren’t uncommon. The last section of trail involves a steep climb to the peak, but the 360-degree views of the Oceano Dunes and coast to the west and surrounding Santa Lucia, Caliente, and Sierra Madre Mountains are worth the effort.

Start on Wittenberg Trail at the end of Lopez Dr. and branch off onto Duna Vista Loop Trail. Duna Vista Spur Trail will lead you to the peak. Hike directly back on the southernmost part of the loop for a slightly shorter route (8 miles) or split off onto the northern section for the full 8.8-mile trek. A day-use fee of $12 per vehicle is required.

This beautiful coastal walk isn’t a challenging one, but give yourself plenty of time to complete it: You’ll want to stop and soak in the wide ocean views, sea stacks, and wetlands. Most of the hike is along bluffs, but opportunities to descend and explore intertidal areas at low tide abound. Expect to see plenty of sea lions and bring your binoculars to try to spot an endangered snowy plover.

Start at the North Ocean parking lot on the edge of Cayucos and take Estero Bluffs Trail along the coast to the end of the park and back.

There’s more to this hike than classic California coastal scrub. In fact, as you climb over rolling hills, you’ll score views of Morro Bay and jagged Morro Rock, a volcanic plug in the estuary, and even drop into shady live oak groves. Volcanic peaks pepper the periphery, and opportunities to spot wildlife abound, including any of hundreds of bird species that follow the Pacific Flyway.

The state park’s trail system is extensive, but this route is a nice loop that touches a lot of the trails on the east side. Start at the parking lot on S Bay Blvd. and take Quarry Trail to Canet Trail, followed by Chumash, Crespi, Park Ridge, and Live Oak Trails. That leads you right back to the parking lot you started in. You can customize the length by combining various side trails, including to the top of Portola Trail, a small summit, which adds about a mile. A state park pass or $8 day-use fee is required.

This rugged trail leads into the Santa Lucia Wilderness within Los Padres National Forest, finally reaching an 80-foot waterfall that streams into an azure pool. Much of the trail slowly climbs through the woods alongside a creek and lush riparian zone in Big Falls Canyon. However, if you continue onward past the trio of falls, your efforts will be rewarded with sunny views of the surrounding Coast Ranges.

Start at Big Falls Trailhead on Upper Lopez Canyon Road and hike to the junction with Hi Mountain Trail. Return the same way. The last section of Lopez Canyon Road to the trailhead can be rough, so 4WD is recommended.

Eat: Gardens of Avila, located within Sycamore Mineral Springs Resort and Spa in San Luis Obispo, offers elegant dining, homegrown ingredients, and garden paths for after-dinner strolls.

Sip: Wineries abound. Oenophiles who appreciate organic and regenerative farming should visit Chamisal Vineyards in Edna Valley.

Explore Town: Los Osos offers welcoming coffee shops, local art galleries, and fine boutiques. Avila Beach is worth exploring for sunny waterfront vibes. Cayucos is home to classic seaside tourist scenes complete with souvenir shops and antique stores.

Campgrounds are in no short supply along Highway 1, and the San Luis Obispo area is no exception. Most of the state parks, like Montaña de Oro, Morro Bay, and Estero Bluffs, have their own developed campgrounds. Morro Beach also has a campground along the coast. Those can be booked online or in person, but they fill up quickly, especially on weekends. During the winter, these campgrounds are closed, so you’ll need to seek out dispersed camping or other lodging options.

Dispersed camping is strictly prohibited along Highway 1. However, some nearby mountain areas allow it. Check out TV Tower Road, Carrizo Plain National Monument, or Williams Hill Recreation Area.

Want to break up your nights outside? You have options. San Luis Obispo and nearby towns have plenty of hotels, BnB’s, and more luxurious accommodations. For a splurge, check out La Lomita Ranch, a chic and colorful former stable with painted tiles, terracotta, and horses in San Luis Obispo.

What the FTC Wants to Know About How Airlines Set Fares

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Airline pricing has come under more scrutiny in recent years after Delta disclosed it was using AI to price some fares and a lawsuit was filed against JetBlue, alleging that the company uses personal data to price airfares. The airline industry has said it uses no personal data to determine pricing.

Airline pricing is once again under scrutiny. Last week, the Federal Trade Commission ordered Delta Air Lines, United Airlines and JetBlue to provide data on how they set fares, as part of a study of whether companies use personal information to set prices.

The agency wants details on the companies’ pricing methods. The companies have 45 days to respond.

“We intend to scrutinize the opaque methodology that some companies in these industries may be using to determine how much consumers pay or how much gig workers are paid for the same product or service,” FTC Chairman Andrew Ferguson said in a statement.

The FTC sent similar orders to Lyft, DoorDash, Uber, Instacart, Target and Walmart, seeking data on pricing and, for the gig companies, on worker pay.

The Federal Trade Commission has ordered Delta Air Lines, United Airlines, and JetBlue to hand over data on how they set fares, part of a broader study into whether companies use personal information to price goods and services. The airlines have 45 days to respond. The same orders went to Lyft, DoorDash, Uber, Instacart, Target, and Walmart, with FTC Chairman Andrew Ferguson saying the agency intends to scrutinize "opaque" pricing methodology. The inquiry lands as fares have risen sharply this year, largely because of surging jet fuel costs, and as airlines expand their use of AI in pricing. Delta, which told investors in 2024 it was working with the Israeli startup Fetcherr and said last year it was pricing about 3% of domestic routes with AI with a goal of up to 25% by the end of 2025, said it does not use personal data to set fares and has never used, tested, or planned individualized pricing. United referred questions to Airlines for America, which said it welcomes the study and that its carriers do not engage in personalized pricing. JetBlue faces a class action filed in April alleging it uses trackers to set prices dynamically. Roughly a dozen states have proposed laws this year limiting how companies collect and use customer search data, with California, Arizona, Hawaii, Maryland, Pennsylvania, and Vermont proposing outright bans on surveillance pricing.

Mamdani's Five Ballot Questions Pass, But Arts Groups Remain Skeptical

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Concerns over landmark sites and lack of public input have dampened trust in Proposal 3, in particular.

Two arts organizations remain concerned that one of New York City's approved ballot measures could weaken review processes over public land usage, leaving areas such as landmark sites vulnerable to hasty sales and without sufficient community input.

All five ballot measures passed with strong support in yesterday's midterms, though the Municipal Art Society (MAS) of New York and the New York Landmarks Conservancy objected to parts of Proposal 3, which expedites the review of eligible projects in three areas: street safety construction, the sale or leasing of city-owned property, and the acquisition of office space for city employees.

Both groups, along with organizations such as the League of Women's Voters, have expressed varying levels of agreement with its three bundled target areas and called for them to be considered separately for independent review.

The measure is one of five put forth by the Commission on Government Efficiency, a 15-member panel assembled by Mayor Zohran Mamdani. All items aim to streamline city operations through revising the City Charter, such as reducing city contractor paperwork and setting aside a rainy-day fund in case of emergency.

"New York has had a strong tradition of public participation and robust debate, and over time, that's been chipped away," Andrea Goldwyn, the director of public policy at New York Landmarks Conservancy, told Hyperallergic.

Goldwyn's primary concern is with the second prong of Proposal 3, which reduces public review when the city sells or transfers unused development rights at landmark sites. Originally, most sales or leases of city-owned property underwent a seven-month review process called the Uniform Land Use Review Process (ULURP) and required City Council's final approval.

Proposal 3 allows projects not believed to have a sizeable impact on the surrounding environment to bypass final City Council approval and go through a 90-day fast lane called the Expedited Land Use Review Procedure (ELURP). Projects approved for ELURP can be concurrently reviewed by community boards and borough presidents, and city-owned landmarked property can be sold without City Council approval. Undevelopable land can additionally be sold to owners of adjacent properties without a public auction.

The proposal also excuses the Department of Transportation (DOT) from notifying elected officials and community boards before starting major projects deemed necessary for safety, and can instead notify those stakeholders when construction commences. Consultations with the Police Department, Fire Department, the Mayor’s Office for People with Disabilities, and the Department of Small Business Services remain mandatory.

"We've gotten calls from people who see new projects going up in their neighborhoods that they didn't know about," Goldwyn continued. "I think we're going to be seeing more of that because people aren't going to have the kind of notification they're used to having."

The final part of the proposal removes hearing requirements and approvals from the City Council and the City Planning Commission about acquiring or leasing office space for city employees, instead leaving it solely to Yume Kitasei, the commissioner for the Department of Citywide Administrative Services. Local community boards and all five borough presidents must still be notified of each proposal.

In an earlier statement, MAS stated that trimming down these review processes "could make it easier to sell or transfer valuable public land and development rights without sufficient safeguards to ensure lasting public benefits."

MAS called for a stronger framework that determines how public lands can be used. Such recommendations include prioritizing land for parks, affordable housing, and other projects that serve the public, rather than selling or leasing land to parties with unclear interests.

"Streamlining government processes should not come at the expense of public accountability or lasting community benefits," the statement adds.

The Best Hot Dogs in Chicago, According to Someone Who Has Tried 'Em All

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you’re looking for the best hot dog in Chicago, you’ve come to the right place. While I can’t tell you how many hot dogs I’ve eaten in my life, I am sure that number is in the many thousands. After high school, I worked at Nick’s Nest, a hot dog stand in my Massachusetts hometown, where I would have one for lunch every day. They’re a favorite food to eat while traveling, and I regularly grab them in my daily life as well, because I live in Chicago, where the hot dog is a local staple.

Served in a steamed poppyseed bun, the Chicago dog has specific toppings: mustard, chopped onions, neon green relish, tomatoes (slices or wedges), pickles (usually spears, sometimes chips), spicy sport peppers, and celery salt. The all-beef dog has a natural casing, which gives a perfect snap when you bite into it. While most Chicago dogs are pretty good, the best ones are constructed so you taste everything in every bite. And you probably know this, but just to be sure: that bite will not include ketchup, as the tangy condiment doesn’t mesh with the other toppings (this is a real if-you-know-you-know thing in Chicago—and you want to be in the know). When you’re in the city, whether you’re siddling up to a hot dog stand or popping into a casual restaurant, order yours as a “Chicago dog” or ask for it “dragged through the garden”—a signal that you know what’s up, and that you want everything on it.

These are the best places to get a hot dog in Chicago, according to a dog-loving resident.

Read our complete Chicago travel guide here, which includes:

It feels very Chicago that you can get an excellent hot dog inside Home Depot. Fixin’ Franks is a family-owned spot with outposts in stores around the city. You’ll find the stands tucked near the exit, where it’s a treat for finishing all your home-repair shopping. There’s a tight, locally sourced menu of hot dogs, Polish and Maxwell Street sausages, Italian beef, and elotes. Get a classic Chicago dog—and if you want to push the envelope, add J.P. Graziano hot giardiniera on top.

1017 W. Irving Park Rd., Lakeview; 1701 W. Lawrence Ave., Ravenswood

While most restaurants follow the dragged-through-the-garden approach, Byron’s, which served their hot dogs at the White House during the Obama administration, takes a different approach. The standard dressing at the 51-year-old stand consists of mustard, relish, onions, tomato, pickle spears, and celery salt, and adds lettuce, green peppers, and cucumber slices (hot peppers are available on request). While this is, admittedly, a lot to maneuver while eating, the salad approach adds a lot of freshness and almost makes it seem like a healthy choice.

1250 S. Union Ave., University Village; 2775 N. Elston Ave., Avondale

Must order: Polish sausage sandwich, pork chop sandwich

Chicago’s hot dog stand culture includes more than just dogs. Jim’s is the best place for two other local beloveds: The Maxwell Street Polish and the pork chop sandwich. The Polish, a garlicky smoked beef and pork link, is generally credited to the stand’s namesake, Macedonian immigrant Jimmy Stefanovic, who took over his aunt and uncle’s stand in 1939. It’s served with mustard, tons of grilled onions, and sport peppers. The pork chop sandwich, served bone-in (bite carefully!) comes with the same garnishes, as does the all-beef hot dog. There’s no seating at the University Village location, which is open from 6 a.m. to 1 a.m., but you can eat at the metal counter or sit on the curb—Jim’s always feels like a party.

Must order: Hot dog, chocolate cake shake

When visitors come to town and want to try Chicago classics, I send them to Portillo’s. The local chain, which Dick Portillo founded in 1963, offers solid versions of local favorites, including hot dogs, Italian beef, and a Maxwell Street Polish. The steamed dog is expertly done and a go-to. Also a go-to is the chocolate cake shake, which is made with—you guessed it—a slice of chocolate cake blitzed into a milkshake. It’s decadently rich, and a true Chicago experience.

3057 N. Ashland Ave., Lakeview; 2449 W. Armitage Ave., Logan Square; 500 W. 35th St., Bridgeport; 4811 Dempster St., Skokie

Must-order: Hot dog and fries, RHR double cheeseburger

With four locations of this locally beloved stand (the O.G. Bridgeport location goes by 35th Street Red Hots), you are never far from one of the city’s best dogs. The links are served Depression-style, which means they have stripped-down toppings: just mustard, onion, relish, and sport peppers, with a pile of fries on top, for a full meal that tops out at just $5.20. Many locals, myself included, feel that Red Hot Ranch’s double smashburger is the best in the city, so get one of those to split as well.

6363 N. Milwaukee Ave., Norwood Park East; 333 S. Milwaukee Ave., Wheeling

In 1948, Maurie and Flaurie Berman opened their drive-in with two signatures. First, the couple appears as 12-foot hot dog statues on the building’s roof—you can’t miss this duo. And second, they serve a hot dog with a point of view: It’s a custom all-beef dog a bit larger than your standard link, and topped with mustard,

a pickle, sport peppers, and chopped onions, plus piccalilli and a pickled green tomato. It comes tucked with crinkle-cut fries in a custom cardboard box, which you can even peep in an episode of The Bear.

Must-order: Chicago-style Duck Inn Dog, fried cheese curds

What happens when you take a Chicago classic and put it in the hands of one of the city’s best chefs? You get Kevin Hickey’s Duck Inn Dog. Every element is made to Hickey’s specs: The juicy beef, pork, and duck fat sausage is grilled, tucked in a brioche poppyseed bun, and dressed with a “supercondiment” of mustard and relish mixed with tomato and onion. It’s finished with a dill pickle, pickled serranos, and celery salt (and perfect with fried cheese curds alongside). This is the glow-up the humble dog deserves.

Much of the love for 43-year-old The Wiener’s Circle is for its heckling servers, led by local institution Poochie Rollins. That experience is one reason to visit the Lincoln Park stand, which is famously open until 4 a.m. on weekends (and 2 a.m. the rest of the week). The other? The excellent food, like the char dog. Most stands steam their dogs, but the charring approach here adds a smoky flavor and crisper texture. Do not miss the fantastic cheese fries, with a generous dollop of tangy cheddar sauce.

Most hot dog stands in Chicago use locally made Vienna Beef links, and it’s worth venturing straight to the source to try one at this cafe, which is located across the street from the factory. Snag a red stool and order the textbook Chicago-style dog and a side of crispy fries. After lunch, peruse the goodies in the shop, which range from cases of hot dogs to hot dog stuffies.

Artur Wnorowski’s casual Polish joint serves some of my favorite sausages in town. All are made in-house: The kielbasa, which he learned to make from his grandfather, is smoked over pecan wood and dressed with caramelized onions, sport peppers, and hazy IPA mustard, while the all-beef dog is available Chicago-style or Polish-inspired. With sauerkraut, onions, sour cream, and a potato and cheese pierogi on top, it’s a nod to the sense of fun Chicago’s hot dog stands deliver.

We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

Cardano Ready to Repeat Its Legendary 2,000% Rally? Pundit Reveals Two Key Price Levels for Next Bull Run

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Cardano’s ADA could be approaching a crucial turning point, with one analyst suggesting its current chart structure bears striking similarities to the setup that preceded its historic 2,000% rally. However, before the next bull-market phase can take shape, ADA must reclaim two crucial price levels that could determine whether another explosive run is on the horizon.

In a recent X post, 360Trader shared Cardano’s weekly chart, highlighting similarities between ADA’s current market structure and the price action witnessed during its previous cycle. Despite the resemblance, the analyst believes the cryptocurrency has yet to confirm a bullish shift, noting that it remains stuck in a consolidation range and needs to reclaim key resistance levels.

360Trader identified $0.25 as the first hurdle to watch, explaining that a swift recovery above this mark would make ADA more interesting to traders. However, Cardano was still trading around $0.1997 at press time, per CoinGecko data, leaving it about 25.19% below the level.

The analyst also pointed to $1.37 as a much bigger milestone, describing it as the threshold that would signal the beginning of ADA’s next bull market phase. This means that even if Cardano manages to reclaim $0.25, it would still have considerable ground to cover before meeting the analyst’s conditions for a stronger bullish trend.

Drawing parallels with Cardano’s previous market cycle, 360Trader sees the possibility of another powerful upward move if the broader crypto market transitions into a bullish phase. The trader, however, stopped short of predicting how high ADA could climb, suggesting that investors may need to remain patient until the next altcoin rally gains momentum.

To put the historical comparison into perspective, a repeat of ADA’s 2,000% surge from its current price of $0.1997 would send the 18th-largest cryptocurrency to roughly $4.19. Such a move would take ADA beyond its former record high of $3.09.

Fulton & Roark Cathedral of Ruins is a dark, sophisticated scent that’s perfect for the season.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

AS SOMEONE WHO spent large chunks of his early twenties driving around the country pretending to be Jack Kerouac (while acting more like Hunter S. Thompson), I’ve spent time in just about every contiguous U.S. state. Some, as you might imagine, are more interesting than others—and Indiana isn’t exactly topping the list. So when I discovered one of my new favorite fall colognes was inspired by Gary, Indiana, I began questioning my own reality. But Fulton & Roark Cathedral of Ruins Extrait de Parfumis that good.

The only thing I know about Gary, Indiana is that it’s where Michael Jackson and the Jackson 5 were from—and they left. But after reading about the inspiration behind Fulton & Roark’s latest scent—and, more importantly, smelling it—I’m wondering if it might be worth a trip. Specifically, the fragrance is inspired by City Methodist Church in Gary, Indiana.

Built in the 1920s, the church is still admired for its grand Gothic architecture. Over the years, its roof caved in, but the stone walls remained intact, allowing vegetation to grow throughout its halls. Equal parts creepy and beautiful, it’s exactly the type of place I’d like to sneak into to see if I could spend a night without freaking out.

More so, its mysterious, earthy, and intriguing yet inviting atmosphere captures exactly the type of vibe I look for in cologne this time of year. After wearing the fragrance for over a month, here’s how it lived up to its inspiration.

Cathedral of Ruins opens with an intense combination of cypress, black peppercorn, and rose, before heart notes of saffron and Peru balsam begin to emerge. After 20 minutes or so, the base notes of leather and frankincense really start to take hold. The result is a woody amber scent that feels lived in and worldly, like cracking open an old copy of The Portrait of Dorian Gray you found at the back of the library.

It’s sophisticated without being pretentious, and noticeable without being loud or overpowering. After applying two to three sprays to the neck and one to my wrists, the cologne maintains most of its strength for seven to eight hours, but lingers much longer. My girlfriend has noticed whiffs of it on my skin as much as 12 hours after applying it.

SHOP FULTON & ROARK CATHEDRAL OF RUINS EXTRAIT DE PARFUM

As someone who was kicked out of his church in middle school (story for another time), I’m not exactly what you’d call a man of faith. However, I am a fan of historic architecture, and that’s something Cathedral of Ruins nails. The combination of frankincense, saffron, and leather gives this fragrance a rustic, sophisticated feel reminiscent of a musky, old library or chic used bookstore in Upstate New York.

There is also, of course, a Gothic backdrop to the scent. I tend to get sad at the beginning of November, as it’s no longer socially acceptable to talk extensively about horror movies throughout the day now that Halloween is over. This cologne is ideal for those of us who know spooky season never ends.

It’s mysterious, alluring, and deep while still being approachable. It’s subtle enough for the office yet interesting enough for the bar. More than anything, it’s perfect for fall, capturing the beauty of falling leaves and cooling temperatures.

Cathedral of Ruins is for the guy who knows spooky season is a year-long commitment. Its woody amber profile blends cypress, pepper, rose, saffron, leather, and frankincense into a dark, sophisticated scent that feels equally at home in a library, bar, or office. It lasts for most of the day while staying noticeable without becoming overwhelming, making it an easy cold-weather go-to.

Make Your Scrambled Eggs Cracker Barrel-Style With This Easy Method (No Butter Required)

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Cracker Barrel's best-selling menu item is a classic pancake, bacon, and egg combo, and no small part of that is because of the eggs. But according to a self-identified employee on Reddit, the old-timey establishment achieves such a delicious breakfast by keeping things simple, cooking them on a griddle with just canola oil — no butter.

When you include abundant fat in scrambled eggs, they slow down the coagulation of protein curds and keep them creamy and rich. By opting for a quick spray of canola oil instead, you can achieve a wildly different texture, allowing those proteins to join together for something a bit more solid and with a slight chew. Griddles also allow maximum surface area contact with direct heat, helping them cook quickly without burning them or making them tough. This is partially why breaking out the griddle is great when making breakfast for a crowd, and you can always add other foods like pancakes and bacon while you prepare the eggs.

Since you won't be able to rely on fat for texture, it's also important to use Cracker Barrel's preferred griddle temperature of 225 degrees Fahrenheit. This is closer to a medium heat, which keeps the eggs fluffy but still not quite creamy. The restaurant also employs one expert tip for quality scrambled eggs: using farm-fresh ingredients. These have a richer flavor to compensate for less fat and firmer whites to help keep the fluffy shape while you eat them.

When a breakfast item is so good but also so simple, a lot of its quality boils down to technique. Cooking with less fat gives you a smaller margin of error, and a griddle can take some getting used to, so keep your eggs in constant motion and don't be afraid to lean on a bit of added water to level up your breakfast.

When you cook scrambled eggs in a small pan, even a small amount will quickly drop its temperature. The same isn't necessarily true for a griddle, especially if you use one with a heavy-duty steel cooktop that retains a ton of heat, but it also responds quickly when the flame is lowered, allowing precise control of temperature throughout cooking. Using two spatulas to continuously move and fold your eggs is a great way to help them cook evenly. Plus, the successive folded layers only build on all that fluffiness and make them fantastic as an ingredient in things like a breakfast sandwich or burrito.

When it comes to keeping eggs fluffy, but not too rich or runny, your best bet is a simple splash of water. Added moisture, but no fat, steams the curds a bit, helping them plump up without growing dense. While too much will make them soupy and flavorless, a tablespoon per three eggs does a great job. The added moisture also helps prevent them from drying out when using something as hot and fast as a large surface area griddle, so it can be a great tool if you've never cooked on a flat-top before.
