Kim Kardashian’s Latest ‘Raspberry Cream’ Nails Are So Unexpected

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The manicure is a sharp departure from her typical style.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Like the rest of her beauty looks, Kim Kardashian’s nails are often bold. She’s been known to sport long, almond- and coffin-shaped styles that grab your attention (she even added a bedazzled “P” to a Barbie pink manicure once in honor of her then-boyfriend Pete Davidson). But at the season 2 premiere of All’s Fair this week, Kardashian stepped out with short, square-shaped nails in an opaque light pink color. We’re dubbing them “raspberry cream” nails.

“It’s a beautiful interpretation of futuristic minimalism, featuring a soft, milky, almost aquatic pink with a delicate translucency,” says celebrity nail technician and creator of eNailCouture Max Estrada. “I believe the decision to keep the nails short was very intentional. The manicure wasn’t necessarily meant to be the focal point. Instead, it allowed the extraordinary jewelry and the other elements of the look to take center stage.”

And while square nails have taken a backseat to more rounded shapes like almond in recent years, Estrada says that when paired with feminine colors like raspberry cream, they can work beautifully. “Square nails have a certain architectural quality, but when the edges are gently softened, as they are here, they can communicate incredible delicacy and softness,” he says. “In this particular manicure, the square silhouette feels refined rather than severe.” Orly manicurist Riyah Martin agrees, calling short and square nails “cute and chic.”

To re-create the look, start with a short, square nail, and buff out the corners. “The idea is to retain that beautiful square architecture while removing any harshness from the edges,” says Estrada. “For the color, I would apply a thin layer of semi-sheer pink, followed by a delicate milky pink to create that ethereal, translucent neo-milky effect.” Add a glossy top coat, and to emulate Kardashian, be sure to finish with sparkly jewelry.

A Guide to the Blofelds

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There’s a lot of talk going around lately about the casting of the new James Bond. Who will play him… Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, CrimeReads favorite/dark horse Josh Bowman? This question is all well and good… but I want to know if this new leg of the franchise is going to have a Blofeld. Because, notoriously, the 007 films can’t keep it together around this guy.

Who is Blofeld? Ernst Stavro Blofeld, Bond’s archnemesis. He’s a megalomaniac who keeps trying to take over the world in one way or another. Oftentimes, his face is hidden. Sometimes, he’s in a wheelchair. Usually, he is petting a cat. One time, he’s Bond’s adopted brother, kind of.

The whole Blofeld situation is a bit nuts, with a total of nine different actors playing him (in various capacities), across the different chapters of the franchise.

A preamble: EON became embroiled in a legal dispute surrounding Thunderball that had serious implications for the different Blofelds. As noted in the book The Battle for Bond by Robert Sellers, Ian Fleming had based his novel Thunderball on a 1959 script for a film that he had written with his associates Jack Whittingham, Kevin McClory and Ernest Cuneo. Apparently, the story changed considerably and the writers went through multiple versions. In March of 1961, McClory, read an advance copy of the novel and contacted Whittingham, with whom he petitioned the courts to slap the novel with an injunction to stop it from being published. The judge who heard the case that month, permitted the novel to continue being published, though he didn’t ban any further legal action from taking place. Fleming suffered a heart attack during this time, but survived.

The suit (McClory and Whittingham v Fleming, Jonathan Cape Ltd and Bryce) went to court in November of 1963, and the case lasted three weeks. Fleming had two more heat attacks during this process. The men settled the case out of court, agreeing that Fleming was to retain the rights to the novel (as long as further editions credited the story as having been created by McClory and Whittingham as well as Fleming), while McClory and Whittingham won film rights. Fleming died of a fourth heart attack the following year.

What does any of this have to do with Blofeld? Walk with me.

First, multiple actors played the role as a continuous character in the Connery era: Anthony Dawson as Blofeld’s body and Eric Pohlmann as his voice in both From Russia with Love (1963) and Thunderball (1965), then Donald Pleasance in You Only Live Twice (1967). This change-up of actors poses no real issue to the films, because we don’t see his face at all in those first two films; the first time we get to see his face, it’s Pleasance. Continuous enough, fine.

Here’s where it gets wacky. Well, wackier. After You Only Live Twice, EON (the company that produces the Bond films) cast a new 007, George Lazenby, to begin a new era of the franchise. Telly Savalas plays Blofeld in that film, On Her Majesty’s Secret Service (1969). But then Lazenby quit, setting the Bond franchise adrift. They brought back Connery to play Bond in 1971’s Diamonds are Forever, and so cast a new Blofeld: Charles Gray.

This was Connery’s last EON Bond film, with the producers bringing on Roger Moore to start a new era with 1973’s Live and Let Die. But the battle between Kevin McClory and the Fleming estate continued; the original 1963-64 settlement gave way to more lawsuits over who owned what. During this time, EON was not allowed to use any of the elements from Thunderball, which seemed to include the character Blofeld and the organization SPECTRE.

EON had a thing or two to say about how little the Bond franchise needed Blofeld. In a bizarre pre-credits sequence in the 1981 Moore film For Your Eyes Only, Bond casually kills Blofeld by using a helicopter to lift him up and drop him (robotic wheelchair, cat, and all) into a smoke stack, before proceeding with its main narrative. That Blofeld also didn’t show his face; his body was played by John Hollis with voicework by Robert Rietty. He’s also not referred to specifically as Blofeld (for legal reasons, obviously). But the message was clear.

Later legal verdicts in the battle for the rights to Thunderball, especially a landmark one in 1983, confirmed that McClory in fact owned numerous elements of the Thunderball story, and therefore the Bond franchise, as well as that he could make his own movie adaptation of Bond elements as long as they were based on the Thunderball film. So he made his own film, Never Say Never Again, that year, casting Max von Sydow as Blofeld, and Sean Connery as Bond.

Blofeld remains absent for the final Moore film A View to a Kill (1985), both Timothy Dalton films, all four Pierce Brosnan films, and the first three Daniel Craig films.

McClory, who continued to sue for rights and royalties to the Bond properties for the rest of his life, died in 2006. His family resolved the issues with the Fleming estate and EON productions, selling the rights to EON’s parent company in 2007, paving the way for Blofeld’s eventual return in Spectre (2015). In that film, and in the final Craig film No Time to Die (2021), he is played by Christoph Waltz, marking the first time that a single actor has played Blofeld (in both body and voice) across two films.

Olivia Rutigliano is an Editor at Lit Hub and CrimeReads. Her other work appears in Vanity Fair, Vulture, Lapham's Quarterly, AirMail, the Los Angeles Review of Books, Public Books, The Baffler, Bright Wall/Dark Room, Politics/Letters, The Toast, Truly Adventurous, and elsewhere. She has a PhD from the departments of English/comparative literature and theatre at Columbia University, where she was the Marion E. Ponsford fellow. She hosts the podcasst "Culture Schlock" at Lit Hub Radio. She is on instagram at @oldebean, twitter at @oldrutigliano, and bluesky at @oliviarutigliano.bsky.social.

God of War: Laufey has Been Delayed for Additional Polishing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

February 2027 is set to be a massive release window, with remakes and new releases of all kinds of major series hitting one after another. Because of this, a few titles have pushed their release dates back, opting instead to give February releases their time and allowing gamers' wallets a bit of time to recover.

Wuthering Waves is one of the biggest Gachas on the planet, and they do far better than most at answering player complaints. Let's go over exactly how!

Santa Monica Studio joins the teams leaning away from February 2027, as the previously planned God of War: Laufey has been delayed to May 20th, 2027. With how important Laufey’s individual performance needs to be, Santa Monica Studio is making the most of the time they have, refusing to fall for the age-old pull of rushing release in order to present the best game possible.

Santa Monica Studio is easily one of the most prestigious teams under Sony, releasing banger after banger with the God of War series. Coming off the announcement of remakes of the original trilogy, Santa Monica revealed a major gamble with God of War Laufey, the first title in the franchise without Kratos as the central playable character.

The game focuses on Kratos’ wife and Atreus’s mother, Laufey, one of the last Norse giants present in the new God of War’s setting. Trapped within a mystical realm of the afterlife, Laufey seems to be taking major notes from the PS2/PS3 God of War titles, pitting its protagonist against a truly magical set of foes with only one way out.

After some of the more grounded presentation and general combat of the last two God of War titles, Laufey seems like a perfect return to form, one that the team is making sure hits as hard as possible. The release date change is disappointing for eager fans, but it does wonders to dodge some other heavy hitters dropping in February.

If Laufey stuck to its original release plan, the game would be battling titans like Fable, Persona 4 Revival, and Metro 2039, all major releases gamers are setting aside cash and time for. Not that God of War Laufey wouldn’t be able to hang, but it's worth taking a step back if the team could afford to.

Being one of Sony’s flagship studios doesn’t mean that Santa Monica is free of danger, if their recent treatment of Suckerpunch is any indication, but ensuring a good release is best both for the team and the consumers. Laufey doesn’t have the immediate pull for long-time players who haven’t kept up with the new God of War titles, so it needs to stand on its own as strongly as possible.

Santa Monica is a team known for absolute polish, and their refusal to be rushed is a great sign that maintaining this quality is a top priority. Too many games have been released in recent years only to be cannibalized by post-launch patches that add functions that should already be there, a mistake Santa Monica Studio is being careful to avoid.

After some of the more grounded presentation and general combat of the last two God of War titles, Laufey seems like a perfect return to form, one that the team is making sure hits as hard as possible.

May is also a much cushier release month in terms of competing releases, with no major drops ready to clash with God of War Laufey’s acclaim. The new title by Santa Monica Studio may feel like a spinoff meant to buy time, but the opportunity to learn more about Laufey, such an impactful character in the new God of War series, is incredibly exciting for any fans of that saga.

Finally getting her side of the story is a phenomenal way of closing the saga for now, allowing Kratos to rest on his laurels while expanding the wildly fantastical side of not only the Norse saga, but the entire worldwide mythos of God of War.

Hyrule has never looked better as they look in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake, and critics seem to agree!

GM Recalls Over 67,000 Chevy Blazer EVs And Cadillac Lyriqs, Vistiqs, And Even Celestiqs For Temporary Loss Of Braking

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

General Motors is recalling 67,380 Chevrolet Blazer EVs And Cadillac Lyriqs, Vistiqs, and even Celestiqs because under specific circumstances, a software defect can cause a temporary loss of braking ability. According to the NHTSA recall report, GM is aware of 20 minor accidents due to the fault, two of which resulted in minor injuries. Fortunately, the condition only arises at low speeds, but any loss of braking is a deeply concerning and dangerous occurrence. Two-wheel-drive Cadillac Lyriqs are not included in the recall, and the only Blazer EV trims involved are SS and Police Pursuit Vehicles.

Cadillac Lyriqs are hit hardest by the recall, which includes 474 2023 Lyriqs, 22,819 2024 Lyriqs, 11,926 2025 Lyriqs, 9,322 2026 Lyriqs, and 44 2027 Lyriqs. The recall also includes 13,627 2026 Vistiqs and 5,175 2027 Vistiqs, as well as 51 2025 Celestiqs, and 31 2026 Celestiqs. (This is one of our best glimpses yet into how many Celestiqs have been sold.) The only Chevrolets included in the recall are 562 2024 Blazer EVs, 2012 2025 Blazer EVs, 1,314 2026 Blazer EVs, and 23 2027 Blazer EVs. VINs are already searchable on NHTSA's website, and the remedy comes in the form of an over-the-air update for owners who have opted in to those, otherwise owners will have to take their vehicles to dealerships to have the software update downloaded.

The conditions that lead to the temporary loss of brake function are very specific. According to the recall report and GM's testing, "when the brakes are lightly applied at low speeds during a sharp turn and the vehicle encounters a bump or depression – such as at the end of a driveway – a momentary release and reapplication of the brakes may cause the software's slip calculation to misinterpret wheel movement. In some instances, this may result in a temporary loss of braking. The phenomenon is unique to certain vehicles equipped with performance all-wheel drive (pAWD, RPO X0E) and is resolved when the brakes are applied again."

GM already recalled 21,469 all-wheel-drive Lyriqs for a similar issue in 2024, but it found that vehicles built with the software changes following that recall and vehicles that already received the software update from that recall are still susceptible to the conditions described in the new recall, and they need the latest update. The latest software update corrects the slip calculation in the vehicle's brake system control module that causes the temporary loss of braking, which coincidentally is the same claim made on the 2024 recall. Hopefully this time it actually fixes the problem.

If you drive one of the cars included in the recall, be aware of the possibility of braking loss, and remember that if you experience braking loss, normal brake function should return on the next brake press. Dealers should already be notified of the recall, but owner notification is estimated to begin on December 14.

House Democrats Plan Crypto Bill With More SEC Power and Trump Ethics Rules

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

House Democrats are discussing a broader crypto market structure bill for the new Congress beginning January 3, 2027.

The proposal could expand Maxine Waters' earlier Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights.

Proposed ethics provisions could restrict crypto business activities involving Trump and other federal officials.

Following the sweep in U.S. midterm elections, House Democrats are actively discussing plans to introduce an overall cryptocurrency market structure bill for the upcoming Congress. According to crypto journalist Eleanor Terrett, the discussions involve expanding Representative Maxine Waters’ earlier Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights.

The plan would also incorporate Democratic provisions previously proposed during negotiations over the CLARITY Act.

In an X post, Eleanor Terrett said House Democrats are discussing plans to turn Representative Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights into a wider crypto regulation bill. The proposal could be introduced when the new Congress begins on January 3, 2027.

🚨SCOOP: House Democrats are discussing plans to expand the Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, @RepMaxineWaters’ amendment to the Clarity Act in 2025, into a broader crypto “market structure” bill next Congress.The proposal would fold in Democratic Clarity Act…

Terrett said three industry sources shared details of the talks with Democratic staff. The new bill could combine Waters’ earlier investor protection rules with changes Senate Democrats discussed with Republicans before the CLARITY Act failed to advance in September.

Waters first introduced the bill in 2025 to give crypto investors better protection and set clearer rules for the industry.

Democrats are now considering using that proposal as the foundation for a wider regulatory framework.

One of the biggest changes under discussion involves the division of authority between the SEC and the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC).

Under the proposal, the SEC could get more power to oversee crypto trading and market activity, while the CFTC could have a smaller role.

This is different from what the crypto industry has wanted, as many companies prefer the CFTC to have more control over crypto markets. The two sides could disagree over this issue when Congress begins discussing the new bill.

Another major part of the discussions involves ethics requirements for elected officials and their immediate families. The proposed rules may prevent senior government officials from starting, promoting, or profiting from crypto businesses while holding public office.

In May 2025, Waters introduced the Stop TRUMP in Crypto Act to limit certain crypto activities involving government officials and their families.

The issue has gained attention after reports that Trump’s crypto businesses made around $1.4 billion. Democrats want to examine his family’s crypto interests and possible conflicts of interest more closely.

Republicans have reportedly called the planned bill a “messaging bill.” They argue it targets Trump’s crypto businesses rather than creating rules both parties can support.

With Democrats set to control both chambers, the proposal could show how the new Congress plans to approach crypto regulation.

The proposal comes as Bitcoin recovers above $81,000. Meanwhile, the crypto market saw nearly $987.33 million in liquidations over 24 hours, with short positions accounting for $566.59 million, or 57.4% of the total.

The documentary follows Oscar-nominated and Emmy-winning actor Gary Sinise‘s decades-long mission to honor America’s fighting men and women, support injured veterans, and stand beside Gold Star families. It’s a calling that was inspired by Sinise’s work playing a Vietnam vet in “Forrest Gump.”

“The Ballad of Lieutenant Dan” will be available on North American digital HD internet, DVD, cable, and satellite platforms starting on November 10, 2026. It features interviews with Sinise, as well as Jay Leno, Tim Allen, Joe Mantegna, Chip Foose, and Mykelti Williamson. The film also follows Sinise as he grapples with personal tragedy after his son, Mac, dies of cancer at the age of 33. “’The Ballad of Lieutenant Dan’ looks into the world of actor and humanitarian, Gary Sinise, and explores how his commitment to veterans and first responders created a community that supported him through a difficult period in his life,” said Beebe. “This is a story about selfless kindness and, I hope, will inspire all of us to do what we each can to uplift our friends, neighbors and communities.”

Flexmls Accelerates Real Estate AI With the Launch of Scout

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Glowing home icon integrated into a microchip on a digital circuit board, representing smart home automation, connected devices, IoT technology, and intelligent digital infrastructure.

FBS—the makers of Flexmls—has announced the availability of Flexmls Scout, an AI assistant that enables real estate professionals to use natural-language search to access trusted MLS information, analyze market activity, and gain actionable insights about their business.

Built directly into the Flexmls platform, FBS noted that Scout combines permissioned MLS data with AI to streamline daily workflows. Scout is available to Flexmls MLS customers as an enterprise solution.This launch marks a key milestone in Flexmls’s strategy to embed AI into the core MLS experience.

Across desktop and mobile applications, FBS stated that real estate professionals can ask Scout to search listings, surface market statistics, access contact and production details, assist with CMAs, and clarify MLS rules and policies, with queries such as:

Unlike general-purpose AI assistants, FBS added that Scout operates within Flexmls’s established security and permissions framework. Users can access only the information they’re authorized to see, while MLS organizations retain control over how their data is accessed and used in accordance with their licensing terms and policies. MLS data is not retained or used to train or retrain AI models.

For more information, visit https://flexmls.com/discover/.

All Guardian Boss Lairs and rewards in The Division 2 Echoes of Central Park

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Echoes of Central Park DLC has modified the endgame experience of The Division 2 with the introduction of the Guardian bosses. Once you’re done with the campaign, you’ll find the mention of five bosses who are located all around the new map.

Once you reach the conclusion of the main story of Central Park, you’ll get to learn more about the Guardians. All five of them used to be SHD Agents, but consumption of something nefarious turned them into monstrosities.

The locations of all five Guardians are highlighted on the image above. You can always find them on the map (as long as you have completed the story).

The table below has information about all the Signature Skills and abilities of all five Guardians.

The Echoes of Central Park DLC offers exclusive skills that will drop when you defeat these Guardians. There are six new variants to obtain by defeating them.

Additionally, you can also get the new Apex Protocol gear pieces. The pieces drop randomly, so you’ll have to kill the Guardians a fair number of times to complete the full set.

Google Adds Home Service Booking & Quote Links To AI Mode via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

AI Mode now links home service searches to booking and quote pages. Here's how businesses reach those pages and what partners say about lead fees.

Google is allowing home service businesses to accept bookings and quote requests generated by AI Mode via Partner Platforms such as the Marketplaces (Angi & Thumbtack) and Field Service Software providers (Housecall Pro & Jobber). On X Wednesday, Google Search Product VP, Robby Stein made a statement regarding the changes and stated that this product is available now in the U.S.

For a plumber or cleaning company, the way in is its field service software or marketplace account. Jobber says its eligible U.S. customers can take those requests now with no lead fees. Housecall Pro also says there are no lead fees, though its release says its integration is coming soon.

Today, we’re making it even easier to research & book home services with direct links in AI Mode through partners like @angi_home @thumbtack @housecall & @GetJobber (and @ServiceTitan coming soon). It’s available now in the U.S.

In the video posted, a searcher is asking to get a handyman to come by and build a bed and dresser the next day. AI Mode identifies local handyman services with online availability along with other service providers who are able to provide a quote. The listing of each service provider includes their rating along with a brief description of what they do. The first of these listings also lists two available times for an appointment.

In May at Google’s I/O conference, Google announced plans to roll out its agentic booking experience to cover additional types of local experiences & services, including direct links to finish booking with the provider. At that same event Google stated that users will be able to ask Google to call businesses for them (in areas such as home repair, beauty & pet care) and that both features would be rolled out nationwide this summer. On Wednesday, Google announced links to named partner companies providing access to booking Home Services.

Jobber announced this week that eligible customers in the United States can be found and booked using AI Mode. According to Jobber’s Help Page, there are certain requirements for businesses to appear in AI Mode. The business must have an active and paid Jobber subscription, a full address listed in Jobber and a Google Business Profile connected to Jobber (listed at the same location), and the business must have assigned a request or booking form to Google, and Jobber defaults to connecting these forms to Google. Trial subscriptions cannot appear in AI Mode.

Once Google identifies a matching profile, a “Book Online” button appears on the business’s Google Business Profile with a link directly to the business’ Jobber booking form. Most times Google will find a match within a few days as stated in the help page.

Requests and bookings made through AI Mode come into Jobber just like another online booking would. There is no lead fee associated with these bookings and they follow the schedule and availability that the business has established. For assessment bookings, they are automatically confirmed by Jobber unless the business has turned on an approval feature that allows them to accept or decline each one individually before it is confirmed. In announcing the integration, Jobber CEO Sam Pillar explained that he believes his company should assist professionals in showing up when customers use AI to search “while keeping ownership of the customer relationships they’ve worked hard to build.”

Housecall Pro states that all of its users will be able to show up in AI Mode at no additional charge. When a Homeowner makes a request, it will be sent to the selected Professional’s Housecall Pro account inbox. Co-Founder & Chief AI Officer of Housecall Pro, Roland Ligtenberg describes it as “One request, one Pro, no lead fee, no bidding war.”

According to Housecall Pro’s Partner page, every one of Housecall Pro’s Professionals are auto-enrolled in AI Mode without having anything to do on their part. The available time slots will be pulled from the Professional’s Calendar, and according to the partner page, no Google Business Profile is required for a Professional to show up in AI Mode. However, the release does say that the integration is coming soon to eligible US Customers.

Angi says homeowners who select an Angi pro that shows up in AI Mode will go straight to that pro’s Angi Profile where they can get more information about the pro and make a quote request. Thumbtack is on Stein’s list however Thumbtack did not announce an AI Mode plan via press releases as of Nov. 4.

For home service businesses, the platform they picked to run jobs or find customers now also affects whether they can take booking and quote requests from Google’s AI search. What it takes to qualify differs by platform, down to whether a Google Business Profile is required.

Jobber’s and Housecall Pro’s no-lead-fee terms contrast with Google’s own Local Services Ads, whose help page lists a customer’s booking request as a lead advertisers are charged for in the U.S. and Canada. Angi’s annual report says pros pay it fees for consumer matches, but its AI Mode announcement doesn’t say whether those fees apply to quote requests that start in AI Mode.

A business cannot rely solely on qualifying for a partner platform to show up; though Stein’s post does not describe how Google determines which businesses to display in AI mode. On the other hand, Jobber’s help page states if a customer collects client reviews and uses a booking form instead of just a request form then that can help have their business recommended by Google AI mode more frequently.

Customers of Jobber can verify whether their Jobber addresses are the same as those entered into their Google Business Profile. Housecall Pro customers should find out whether their account qualifies with Google AI mode, and Angi pros can check their Angi profile, because according to Angi, the profile information on Angi is where Google’s AI mode sends homeowners to choose from.

Stein listed ServiceTitan as coming soon. A business on none of the listed platforms has no route to direct bookings through AI Mode.

Google’s help page on checking local availability in AI Mode, doesn’t mention partner links. For the latest updates, keep an eye on that page and each partner’s eligibility rules.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

Breaking news and insights to help you lead search strategies.

Breaking news and insights to help you lead search strategies.

Breaking news and insights to help you lead search strategies.

In a world ruled by algorithms, SEJ brings timely, relevant information for SEOs, marketers, and entrepreneurs to optimize and grow their businesses -- and careers.

Southwest’s New Platinum Status Adds Too Little—10 Ways Rapid Rewards Should Fix Its New Program

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Southwest Airlines is adding Platinum status to Rapid Rewards, but someone who already has A-List Preferred and Companion Pass won’t get much new. A third checked bag, earlier boarding, and a higher advertised earning bonus (offset by lower base-earning on higher fares) are very modest additions considering they’re launching a new top tier. Gold, the replacement for A-List Preferred, already gets the same seats. The 2027 overhaul makes things simpler by unifying qualification for elite status and Companion Pass. And they’re adding award travel on Icelandair, China Airlines and Etihad Airways beginning in the spring, including in premium cabins. We don’t yet know redemption pricing, rules or surcharges, so we don’t know how much value those will deliver. I’ve argued that Southwest’s changes follow from one another. Broader distribution puts their fares alongside everyone else’s. That creates pressure to unbundle with basic economy and paid seats and bags. Selling better seats requires assigned seating. Once they’ve given up the old differences, they need more of the benefits that make other airlines worth choosing. Southwest is inexorably becoming like the rest of the industry, but – they insist – with friendler crews. They at least need to become like everyone else without offering less. I offered ten ways to improve Rapid Rewards in 2022. Some, including partner airline redemptions and stronger credit card earning toward status, are finally being addressed. Here’s what I’d do next. Deliver Elite Members’ Bags First Southwest baggage delivery can be slow. That was easier to tolerate when checking bags was free for everyone. Once they’re charging for the service, standing around a carousel feels different. They should offer priority baggage handling for elites, with Gold and Platinum bags among the first delivered. This requires actually sorting and loading the bags so they come out first, not just a colored tag. And back it with a delivery guarantee. Delta offers 2,500 miles when checked bags take more than 20 minutes from gate arrival to the carousel. Alaska has a similar guarantee (and was actually first). Southwest could start with a guarantee for its top elites. I’d rather get my first bag promptly than have a free third one. Give Platinum Members Priority For An Empty Middle SeatSouthwest doesn’t have first class upgrades to offer. It can still make sitting in coach better. When a Platinum member selects a window or aisle in a regular legroom row, hold the adjacent middle seat out of routine seat assignments until it’s needed. Keep selling the flight, and release the seat at the gate if passengers need it. This would determine who gets the empty seat when a flight isn’t full. Someone is going to have extra space beside them. Give the customer who earned the highest status the best chance of being that person. Limiting this to ordinary coach would avoid taking some premium seats they’re trying to sell out of inventory. An empty middle can be better than a few additional inches of legroom. Introduce Lifetime Elite StatusAmerican, Delta, United and Alaska all recognize lifetime flying. Southwest now should, too. I’d base on miles or flight segments since Southwest has never had a long haul netowrk to rack up 20,000 flown miles on a roundtrip. For instance, 1,000 one-way trips could earn lifetime Silver, 2,000 could earn lifetime Gold, and 3,000 lifetime Platinum. I don’t know whether Southwest has the database to support historical flying like this. Give Elites An Award Rebate Make points worth more to the customers who earn status. A 10% rebate on Southwest awards for Gold and 15% for Platinum would make their points more valuable, and could be more easily managed than an award discount. Some Southwest credit cards already provide annual 10% or 15% discount codes. This is especially important because the new program cuts earning on expensive fares. As I laid out in comparing the new tiers, a Choice Extra ticket earns an A-List Preferred member 28 points per eligible dollar today, versus 22 for a Platinum member next year. Let Families Pool Points Free Southwest sells family travel and has one of the best benefits for traveling with someone else. Yet its program rules prohibit members from pooling points. Let a household combine its balances without paying a transfer fee. You can already use your points to book someone else’s ticket. The problem is having enough points across several accounts, but not enough in any one account for the award you want. JetBlue has offered free pooling for groups of up to seven people for years. United has family accounts, and this is a big feature in international progams. Count Award Flights Toward Status A flight paid for entirely with Rapid Rewards points doesn’t count as a qualifying flight. Southwest should change that, like Delta and Alaska do, and include partner awards as that part of the program develops. Southwest wants people to earn points from its credit cards and partners, then use them to fly. Treating that journey as less valuable to the airline makes no sense. Make Companion Pass More Useful When Traveling With Different People Companion Pass remains Southwest’s strongest benefit. But the new program keeps the old restrictions: one designated companion at a time, three changes per calendar year, and existing companion reservations must be canceled before switching people. Let Platinum members designate a small group, perhaps three people, and choose one companion for each trip. A spouse on one trip, a child on another. Still one companion per flight, still traveling with the member, with limits on changing the roster. I’d also let Platinum members use Companion Pass for an adjacent empty seat when traveling alone, an idea I included in my earlier wishlist. This would be a confirmed seat reservation, consuming inventory just as a companion booking does. A member whose travel is mostly business shouldn’t need to bring another person on the trip to get substantial value from the program’s biggest reward. Allow Confirmed Same-Day Changes On Basic Fares Southwest already lets elites use their seating benefits on Basic. That’s a real advantage over competitors, and it extends to family members on the same reservation. But even the new Platinum tier won’t receive free confirmed same-day changes on Basic. Members can stand by. They need to buy Choice or higher to confirm the change without paying a fare difference. Give Gold and Platinum confirmed changes into available seats on the day of travel, regardless of their original fare. Keep the same origin and destination, and let the member choose an available itinerary that gets them there sooner or works better with their schedule. Southwest already provides the service on more expensive fares, here earned status would supply it. Make Southwest Status Useful On Its Airline Partners The international redemption announcement addresses not being able to use Southwest points for long haul travel. The awards need solid pricing and inventory. I expect that we’ll eventually see reciprocal status recognition, but that needs to come quickly: priority check-in, baggage allowances, boarding and seat benefits and lounge access would make Gold and Platinum valuable even when member travels beyond Southwest’s route map. Give Platinum A Choice Of Benefits Southwest is building Icon Lounges, with openings planned to begin in late 2027. Access has been announced through a forthcoming premium Chase card. They haven’t announced a Platinum lounge benefit. Give members an annual choice when they earn Platinum: perhaps some lounge passes which could encourage them to try the product and get the card, or to share the passes with others. They might choose a travel credit or certificates that let friends and family select extra legroom seats when the member isn’t traveling. Southwest is already introducing two milestones, at 20,000 and 95,000 qualifying points which are below Platinum. The earlier milestone’s Preferred seat certificates aren’t extra legroom upgrades and can’t be gifted, so certificates for a friend to choose a better seat would add something different. They should continue with milestones above platinum, and continue offering choice benefits. Southwest Airlines is remaking itself in the image of the rest of the airline industry – with the same kinds of restrictions, but also adding many of the same amenities. They will have three elite tiers, but those tiers need to be competitive. I want Southwest to succeed. Are these things that would do it for you? What’s missing from my list? Topics on this pageFrequent-flyer programLoyalty programRapid RewardsAlaskaAlaska AirlinesChaseChina AirlinesDelta Air LinesEtihad AirwaysGoldIcelandairJetBlueJPMorgan ChasePlatinumSilverSouthwest AirlinesUnited Airlines+14 more More From View from the Wing

So what you’re saying is that if I spend $75k on the CSR I can get the amazing benefits that come with Southwest A-List / Silver status?

>Count Award Flights Toward Status Southwest has done this a couple times in past years. It makes sense because Southwest has been paid for the points and Southwest recognizes revenue when they are flown.

>Introduce Lifetime Elite Status I would like to think my 2000+ flights count for something, even though I know they don’t.

>Allow Confirmed Same-Day Changes On Basic Fares This would be great but it won’t happen because it would reduce revenue by up to 50% on short haul trips booked in advance. Southwest has fired its loyal low-fare customers and does not want to re-hire them.

One improvement unrelated to elite status would be to eliminate or somehow mitigate expiration of travel funds. I have been booking on points to avoid having to worry about funds expiration. If funds expiration were eliminated I would have booked those flights on cash fares. I believe that Southwest is foolish to count breakage as revenue without accurately estimating how many cash fare sales are lost because of funds expiration.

Xbox Announces Two New Methods To Play Games Through The Cloud

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

"Starting today, we’re rolling out two previews for XBOX Cloud Gaming."

Team Xbox continues to make improvements to playing games through the cloud, and they've just announced two new methods for doing just that - both launching in "preview" form later today.

The first of these is the ability to use your phone as a controller, which will be available across Android and iOS on supported Smart TVs (including certain Amazon, LG, Samsung and other models) in this preview.

To access this, you'll just need to turn on the "Preview Features" in the Xbox app on your supported TV:

To try it, turn on Preview Features under Settings > Account in the latest supported Smart TV experience. When you launch a game without a physical controller connected, you’ll see an option to use your phone for that session.

The second new method is something called "cloud connect", which allows you to connect using Wi-Fi to reduce input latency in Xbox Cloud Gaming titles. The catch here is that it only works with the new Xbox Nano Controller for now, and even then it "won’t work with every cloud gaming experience yet" during the preview phase.

Here's a bit more about it, along with an image:

Players in the U.S. can preview cloud connect with the XBOX Nano Controller starting today. Instead of a Bluetooth connection to your TV, cloud connect uses your Wi-Fi to connect directly to the cloud server running your game. That can help reduce input latency, so gameplay may feel more responsive. Cloud connect is still an early test, so it won’t work with every cloud gaming experience yet. For now, you can try it on supported Smart TVs and the web.

For what it's worth, it's been rumoured by Windows Central that the "cloud connect" feature will become part of all Xbox controllers in the future, but for the time being it remains exclusive to the Nano.

So, there you go! It seems these will remain in a preview state for a decent amount of time as the team aims to "shape the experience", so be sure to provide feedback if you have any improvements in mind.

We'll leave you with a related story about the new Nano controller that released today down below.

News Xbox Nano Controller Gets Its First Reviews, And A Secret Hidden Feature Is Revealed

Interested in checking out either of these two new Xbox Cloud Gaming methods? Tell us in the comments.

Fraser is the News Editor at Pure Xbox, where he spends his time reporting on the biggest stories in the world of Xbox and beyond.

It's amazing how well the younger generations handle touch controls. They play on touchscreens without any trouble and even prefer them to traditional controllers. For me, there's no going back, take away the feedback from physical buttons, and I don't know how to play anything.

From Trail Access to Hunting Rights, Here’s How America Voted on Public Lands

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A bull elk stands in an alpine meadow in Colorado's Rocky Mountains. Colorado voters rejected Amendment 83, which would have established a constitutional right to hunt and fish and designated those activities as preferred methods of wildlife management. (Photo: Mark de Leeuw/Getty)

Trail access, hunting rights, and how to spend gear sales taxes are just a handful of the public lands topics Americans voted on during the November 3, 2026, midterm election.

Colorado voters rejected a proposal to protect hunting and fishing in the state constitution, while Massachusetts approved a plan to use existing sporting-goods tax revenue for conservation. Elsewhere, voters weighed in on funding for parks, wildfire prevention, protected open space, and public access to waterways.

Here are seven election developments that could affect how Americans use the outdoors.

Colorado voters rejected Amendment 83, an effort to establish a constitutional right to hunt, fish, and harvest wildlife using “traditional methods.” The amendment did not define what “traditional methods” meant. However, if approved, Amendment 83 would have designated hunting and fishing as the state’s constitutionally preferred methods for managing game species.

Rejecting Amendment 83 preserves the state’s current regulatory system, keeping wildlife policy decisions under the authority of the legislature and the Colorado Parks and Wildlife Commission.

As of this publication, Colorado voters are still waiting on final counts for Proposition 137, a measure that would raise an estimated $175 million in annual conservation funding. If approved, the decision would redirect existing sporting-goods sales tax money toward wildfire mitigation, watershed protection, wildlife habitat, state parks, and trail maintenance.

Colorado limits how much tax money the state can keep, and it generally refunds any excess to taxpayers. Proposition 137 would also allow the state to keep sporting-goods tax revenue for conservation, which could mean smaller refunds in some years and less money for other state programs in others.

Official results have not been published yet. Outside will update this article accordingly.

Massachusetts voters approved creating the Nature for All Fund, which would tap existing sales tax revenues from sporting goods, recreational vehicles, and golf courses.

The fund could receive up to $65 million a year to protect forests, waterways, and wildlife habitat, and to improve parks and trails. Any money left over at the end of the year would stay in the fund rather than return to the state’s general budget, and state lawmakers would still decide how much to spend each year.

Charleston County voters in South Carolina backed extending a half-cent sales tax projected to generate up to $4.25 billion over 25 years.

The measure sets aside $690 million to protect open space, split equally between urban and rural areas. The money could help the county buy land for parks, preserve farmland, and create more places for people to access the water, according to the ballot language. The tax will also fund bike paths and pedestrian improvements through its separate transportation budget.

County officials will still need to approve specific land purchases and projects.

In Sarasota County, Florida, voters supported two complementary measures to protect natural areas and public parks. The first extends an existing property tax through 2049, while the second allows the county to borrow up to $250 million to buy and maintain conservation land.

The county will use money from the extended property tax to help pay back the loans. Together, the measures will help protect wetlands, forests, wildlife habitat, and public parks along Florida’s Gulf Coast.

California voters in Napa County favored a half-cent sales tax that would raise an estimated $23 million annually over 18 years, dividing proceeds between wildfire preparedness programs and the Napa County Regional Park and Open Space District.

The funding could help clear brush and other vegetation that fuels wildfires, protect water supplies, and maintain parks and natural areas.

Official results have not been published yet. Outside will update this article accordingly.

Rhode Island voters cleared the $55 million Green Economy Bond, which will help pay for improvements to state parks, beaches, campgrounds, and other outdoor spaces.

The funding includes $12 million to improve parks, beaches, and campgrounds, $7 million to restore Narragansett Bay and nearby waterways, and $3 million to protect open space. Additional money will support local recreation areas and boating facilities, while $25 million will help communities prepare for flooding, coastal erosion, and other climate-related damage.

A 1,600-Year-Old Artistic Marvel Seemed Physically Impossible to Recreate. Until One Scientist Did.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

It took the better part of two millennia to figure out the science behind how this ancient glass cup could change colors.

Roughly 1,600 years ago, ancient Romans crafted one of the most pristine—and scientifically advanced—works of glass art, a cup that shifts colors between jade green and ruby red. We’ve never adequately replicated the creation, but that’s all changing thanks to the scientific process used in a University of Sheffield project aimed at mimicking the Romans.

“Although if the Romans could have seen me experimenting,” said Lisa Hollands, a team leader at the university’s School of Chemical, Materials, and Biological Engineering, in a statement, “they’d probably laugh and ask: ‘Why does she not know how to make this?’”

Roman color-changing glass first appeared in the fourth century C.E., and the Lycurgus Cup offers the most glamorous example of it. Now on display at the British Museum, it’s one of 40 surviving glass cage cups. Its exterior depicts the mythical Thracian king Lycurgus ensnared in vines. The cup appears green in reflected light and red when light shines through it.

It’s a spectacular museum piece, but it’s long puzzled glassmakers and historians. How did the Romans create this color-shifting trick, now known as the dichroic effect? And why has every modern attempt to reproduce it failed? The leading explanation is that Roman artisans embedded trace amounts of gold and silver in the glass, forming nanoparticles thousands of times smaller than a grain of sand. Because these particles are smaller than the wavelengths of light, they don’t just let light pass through. They absorb and scatter certain colors depending on their size and shape, so the glass looks different depending on how light hits it.

“I knew that making a glass of this type was likely to be a complex task, as it is not just a case of adding nanoparticles to glass,” Hollands said. “I’ve studied and worked with glass for over 25 years, so I used the knowledge and experience I’ve gained, coupled with a modern scientific understanding of what I was trying to achieve.”

Hollands partnered with Caroline Jackson, a leading Roman glass expert, to learn about Roman-era glassmaking and to understand the materials they might have used. “Then came the more difficult part of trying to get small amounts of gold and silver into the glass in the right form,” she said.

Hollands experimented with different furnace types, starting materials with varying oxygen levels, and even sugar. Eventually she hit on a recipe that works: Her glass looks opaque green in reflected light and glows red when light shines through.

This magical effect occurs because when gold and silver join the molten glass mixture, nanocrystals grow. Because the nanoparticles are smaller than the wavelengths of light, Hollands told The Guardian, the electrons in their atoms can block certain colors under the right conditions. “So, for this glass, if light is shone through it, the gold and silver particles block blue and green light, only allowing reds and oranges to pass through,” she said. “If light is reflected off the surface, the particles have the effect of scattering the light, giving the opaque green color we see.”

With the recipe finally cracked, the team asked Elliot Walker to use it in a contemporary sculpture. Walker is one of the UK's foremost glass artists and won season two of Netflix’s Blown Away. “It is a very rare thing to have the opportunity to work with glass developed from ancient mixtures and recipes, and it is always a very exciting one,” Walker said. “You can really get an idea of how the glass makers of the past would have adapted to this sort of glass. It’s like shaking their hands.”

Walker gave the sculpture a classical form to echo the Lycurgus Cup, and he included a blown-glass element to show off its color-shifting effect. “The piece is a blend of the Lycurgus story and the Farnese Atlas sculpture,” he said, “with the globe replaced by a cup.”

The new creation goes on display at the university’s Festival of the Mind on November 6 before shifting permanently into the school’s Turner Museum of Glass.

The Sheffield team said that although the recipe is mainly meant for decorative glass, the science behind it could lead to new photonic materials, which control light, and to advanced glasses for storing radioactive waste. Already, the project has contributed to research on immobilizing complex nuclear waste in glass and understanding the conditions that cause crystals to form, which helps maximize how much waste can be safely locked away.

“What Lisa and Elliot have managed to achieve has surpassed all our expectations,” said Christina Metcalfe, the school’s Molecular Photonic Breadboards project manager. “The incredible historic narrative rally captures people’s imaginations, and I like to think we’ve created something that people will marvel at for years to come.”

Tim Newcomb is a journalist based in the Pacific Northwest. He covers stadiums, sneakers, gear, infrastructure, and more for a variety of publications, including Popular Mechanics. His favorite interviews have included sit-downs with Roger Federer in Switzerland, Kobe Bryant in Los Angeles, and Tinker Hatfield in Portland.

The AI Race Is No Longer About Better Models. Here’s Where the Real Value Is Being Created.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

As AI models mature, the true battleground has shifted from raw intelligence to real-world deployment.

Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.

For the past three years, the AI story was a race to build a better model. Every few months, a company released one that was faster, smarter and cheaper. In 2026, the story changed. The biggest AI announcements of the year were not new models at all. They were new companies built for one purpose — putting engineers inside other companies to make AI work there.

In May 2026, OpenAI launched a new company for this, backed by more than $4 billion. Its engineers move into a customer’s business, learn how the work really gets done and stay until the AI works in day-to-day use. Days earlier, Anthropic announced a similar company of its own, built for mid-sized businesses that do not have enough technical staff to do this work themselves.

These engineers have a name: forward-deployed engineers. The idea started at Palantir. Instead of selling software and wishing the customer luck, the engineer sits with the customer’s own team and connects the AI to the company’s real information and real way of working. They are not judged on advice or presentations. They are judged on one thing — whether the system still works when real customers, real orders and real problems show up.

This is a real change. Big technology companies were never supposed to work this way. The old model was clean — build a product once and sell it to everyone. When the world’s leading AI companies spend billions to put their own people inside customer businesses, they are admitting something important. The scarce thing is no longer the AI itself. It is people who can make AI work inside a real business.

The size of the spending makes the point. Read together, the moves of this one spring form a clear pattern: Deployment has become the main event in AI in 2026, with billions aimed at the same job. These leaders now believe value is created on the day a system starts working inside a business — and keeps working.

This change did not come from a theory. It came from three hard years of test projects. An AI test looks wonderful in a demonstration — the data is clean, the users are friendly, and the results are impressive. Then the system meets the real business, and it struggles.

Real business information is messy. The same customer appears three times under three slightly different names. Prices are out of date. Every department keeps its own version of the truth. An AI system cannot spot any of this. It trusts what it finds and acts on it, quickly and confidently. That is where most AI projects quietly die — not in the demonstration, but in the first months of real use.

The numbers back this up. Gartner predicts that through 2026, companies will give up on 60% of AI projects that are not built on data that is ready for AI — data that is accurate, current and agreed across the business. In the same research, 63% of companies said they either do not manage their data the way AI needs, or are not sure that they do.

Gartner also warns that more than 40% of AI agent projects will be canceled by the end of 2027, because of rising costs, unclear results and weak controls. The AI itself is not on that list of reasons. The problem is the ground it stands on — the data, the daily process and the basic rules around it. That ground is exactly what a forward-deployed engineer is sent in to fix.

I have led data and AI teams for years, and this matches what I have seen. Businesses did not learn that AI was weak. They learned that their own house was not ready for it. Deployment is the work of getting the house ready.

So where is this heading? Gartner expects that by the end of 2026, 40% of business software will come with built-in AI agents that handle specific tasks, up from less than 5% in 2025. By 2028, it predicts, at least 15% of everyday work decisions will be made by AI on its own.

If even part of that happens, the key question for a business changes. It will not be which AI you buy — AI will keep getting better and cheaper, whoever sells it. The question will be whether your business is ready. Is your information clean and agreed in one place? Do you have clear examples of what a good result looks like? Are the rules clear for what AI may do alone and what a person must approve?

There is a second shift inside this one. The team that works inside a business every day learns how it truly runs — where time is wasted, where mistakes happen and where money is made. That knowledge decides what gets bought next. This is why the labs moved so fast. The engineer inside the building is becoming the most important seat in AI, and no one wants a rival sitting in it.

For founders and smaller companies, this is good news. The old race rewarded whoever could spend the most on technology. The new one rewards whoever understands a customer’s real work the deepest. Many of the important AI companies of the next decade will not build models. They will make AI work, industry by industry and business by business.

New models will keep making headlines, and they deserve to. But the future of AI will not be settled in a laboratory. It will be settled in offices, warehouses, clinics and shops, by people doing the unglamorous work of making AI succeed in real life. That work is deployment. And deployment is the main event now.

For the past three years, the AI story was a race to build a better model. Every few months, a company released one that was faster, smarter and cheaper. In 2026, the story changed. The biggest AI announcements of the year were not new models at all. They were new companies built for one purpose — putting engineers inside other companies to make AI work there.

In May 2026, OpenAI launched a new company for this, backed by more than $4 billion. Its engineers move into a customer’s business, learn how the work really gets done and stay until the AI works in day-to-day use. Days earlier, Anthropic announced a similar company of its own, built for mid-sized businesses that do not have enough technical staff to do this work themselves.

These engineers have a name: forward-deployed engineers. The idea started at Palantir. Instead of selling software and wishing the customer luck, the engineer sits with the customer’s own team and connects the AI to the company’s real information and real way of working. They are not judged on advice or presentations. They are judged on one thing — whether the system still works when real customers, real orders and real problems show up.

Epic Adds Features to Fortnite Crew Subscription, Removes Others

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

Fortnite’s self-perpetuating freemium economy is built around several forms of transactions and subscriptions. The most prominent of these are the Item Shop, where you buy cosmetics piecemeal for V-Bucks, and the Battle Pass, which you buy once per season to gain access to a set sequence of unlockables by leveling up.

In addition to these, though, Fortnite also offers the Fortnite Crew subscription service which, for a recurring monthly fee, gets you access to the current Battle Pass, other content passes like the OG Pass, Music Pass, and for fans of video game LEGO kits, the LEGO Pass, plus a monthly payout of 800 V-Bucks. It costs more than the Battle Pass by itself, but all the extra content is very attractive, especially if you don’t already have a bunch of skins to flip through.

During the Treasure Hunters event, players have a chance to uncover a token that gives near-unfettered access to the item shop for two weeks.

As Epic Games announced on the official Fortnite news blog today, though, some changes are coming to the Crew Subscription starting on December 5. Some of these changes are additive, and may help to improve the value of the subscription gameplay-wise, but they’re accompanied by some subtractions, which could harm the overall bang-for-buck depending on your perspective.

Let’s start with the good news. Firstly, the price of Fortnite Crew is staying the same at $11.99 monthly, and you’ll still get access to the current Battle Pass, Music Pass, LEGO Pass and Rocket Pass Premium.

The price of Fortnite Crew is staying the same at $11.99 monthly.

The people gave Sweeney and Epic all the attention and support it needed when it rose up to Apple. As a reward, players got a significant price hike.

In addition, all Crew members will begin receiving a flat 20% bonus to all XP earned while playing the game, with a 10% XP bonus also given to any squad members in your party, even if they’re not Crew members themselves. These boosts don’t stack, though, so you won’t get any additional XP even if you have other Crew members in your squad.

So, that’s the good news, now what’s the bad news? The first piece of bad news is that Crew Pack Outfits, one of the most distinctive benefits to getting a Crew sub, are being discontinued. The final Crew Pack Outfit will be distributed in December, with no further packs to follow. On the bright side, if there were any Legacy Styles you didn’t have for your Crew Pack Outfits, which would normally be obtained by staying subbed for sequential months, they’ll instead just be given to you in bulk.

The second piece of bad news is that, at least for the time being, OG Pass is ending, with an apparent “dramatic conclusion” occurring on December 5 with the end of Chapter 1: OG Season X. This means you won’t be able to redeem the large quantity of skins and other assorted cosmetics that this pass would normally provide.

That’s definitely a big blow to the overall value factor of a Crew subscription, though according to Epic’s news post, “The Next Era of Fortnite OG drops in late 2027,” so perhaps the OG Pass will make a comeback at some point.

Epic Games has laid off over 1,000 employees and cut three game modes from Fortnite, raising questions about the company's (and Fortnite's) future.

BMW Just Went 186 MPH In The New Alpina 7 Series

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Thanks to spy shots, we already knew BMW was hard at work cooking up the next Alpina B7 and that it'll have a V8 that sounds pretty great. The brand has now come out with official footage of the car doing high-speed testing at the Nardò test track, exceeding 186 mph. What, did you think the special label version of BMW's flagship sedan would be slow? For reference, the last-gen Alpina B7 already had a top speed of 205 mph, so 186 was frankly table stakes. That said, the speed itself arguably isn't what BMW is flexing her — it's the peace with which that speed is achieved.

"Speed has always been baked into the Alpina brand's DNA," said BMW Alpina vice president Oliver Viellechner. "However, we are not just interested in top speed alone. What matters is how natural, stable and confident it feels to drive at that speed." Internally, the brand apparently calls these cars "distance-shrinking" machines; autobahn weapons made to cover literal continents in great speed, style, and serenity.

You might recognize the name Nardò, by the way, from "Nardo Gray," that primer-like paint color popularized by Audi. The Nardò Ring, a 7.8-mile test track in southern Italy owned by Porsche and shaped like a perfect circle, is where that comes from.

Other confirmed details now include Alpina-signature 20-spoke lightweight wheels (peeping at the sidewall shows a diameter of 22 inches) wearing specially made tires that feature an extra layer of foam inside allowing for peak high-speed stability and comfort. Four oval exhaust tips emit a "rich, deep sound," and BMW says max torque out of that V8 is available "across virtually the entire rev range," while engine management software and throttle mapping is tweaked for smoothness over aggression. Gearbox tuning, too, has been revised to accommodate the bump in horsepower and engine speeds.

This new B7 (or whatever BMW will choose to call the next Alpina-ized 7 Series) will be the first production car made under the joint BMW Alpina brand. BMW announced that it had acquired Alpina back in 2022, but didn't take full control over the tuner until the beginning of this year. The new BMW Alpina 7 Series will be unveiled next year.

ChatGPT can now make animated GIFs with OpenAI’s Images 2.6

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Users can turn a prompt or photo into a GIF, then change its speed and edit single frames.

The ChatGPT app open on an iPhone, its dark start screen showing the OpenAI logo.

OpenAI is adding animated GIFs to ChatGPT with a new image model, Images 2.6. Users can make a GIF from text or a photo and edit its frames. Developers get two new API models, and OpenAI says people now make over 4 billion images a week.

OpenAI is rolling out animated GIFs in ChatGPT alongside a new image model, Images 2.6, The Verge reported on Wednesday. Users can turn a written prompt or an uploaded photo into a GIF.

Once a GIF is made, users can change how fast it plays, look at and edit single frames, and share it. Until now, ChatGPT could not make a GIF in one step. The feature arrives months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its AI video app, in March.

OpenAI says Images 2.6 also makes image generation in ChatGPT faster. It is rolling out to all ChatGPT and Codex users on the web, desktop and mobile.

Developers get two new API models: GPT-Image-2.6 Flare for speed and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst for quality. They follow the split OpenAI introduced with Images 2.5 in September, when Adobe put one of its models into Firefly.

Use keeps growing. OpenAI says people now make more than 4 billion images a week across ChatGPT and its API, up from 3 billion in September. The figures are OpenAI’s own and cover all image use, not only GIFs.

OpenAI has updated its image tools often this year. Images 2.0, launched in April, was its first image model that reasons before drawing. Last month, OpenAI began testing ads inside image generation in the US.

The Real Ark of the Covenant May Be Hiding in Ethiopia—and Only One Man Alive Is Allowed to See It

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

According to an ancient Ethiopian epic, the biblical relic was stolen in a 3,000-year-old temple heist—and now sits in a tiny chapel guarded by a single monk for life.

Next to the Church of Our Lady Mary of Zion in the ancient city of Axum, Ethiopia, stands a small, fenced-off sanctuary called the Chapel of the Tablet. Entry is forbidden to everyone on Earth except one man: a lone monk appointed for life to watch over its contents.

According to believers, he’s guarding one of history’s most sacred and sought-after relics: the Ark of the Covenant.

In the Hebrew Bible, the Ark is described in precise detail: a rectangular chest of acacia wood overlaid inside and out with gold, measuring roughly four feet long by two feet wide and high, and crowned with two golden cherubim with outstretched wings. Forged at Mount Sinai to house the Ten Commandments and serve as God’s literal throne on Earth, the Ark guided the Israelites through the wilderness, toppled enemy armies, and eventually became the sacred centerpiece of King Solomon’s Temple in Jerusalem. Then, during the Babylonian destruction of Jerusalem in 586 B.C.E., this ultimate seat of divine power vanished from the biblical record without a trace.

For millennia, its disappearance has fueled endless archaeological quests, conspiracy theories, and pop-culture touchstones like 1981’s Raiders of the Lost Ark. Yet according to the Kebra Nagast (“The Glory of the Kings”), a revered 14th-century Ethiopian national epic, the Ark was never lost at all. Instead, it was smuggled out of Jerusalem nearly 3,000 years ago by Menelik I, the son of King Solomon and Makeda—better known outside Ethiopia as the Queen of Sheba.

According to the epic, Menelik grew up in Ethiopia before traveling to Jerusalem in his early twenties to meet his father, carrying a royal ring Solomon had left with his mother as proof of his lineage. Solomon recognized the young man immediately—he was the spitting image of his grandfather, King David—and begged his favored son to stay and inherit the throne of Israel.

Menelik insisted on returning home to rule his mother’s kingdom. Reluctantly, Solomon agreed, but on one condition. The firstborn sons of Jerusalem’s highest-ranking nobles, priests, and military commanders were ordered to accompany Menelik as his new royal court to establish a “second Israel” in Ethiopia.

That decree sealed the Ark’s fate. Devastated at the thought of leaving the presence of God behind, the young elites conspired to pull off an ancient inside job. The heist was masterminded by Azariah, son of the High Priest Zadok. Prompted by an angel in a vision, Azariah commissioned an exact wooden replica of the Ark. Under cover of night, he and his co-conspirators slipped into the Temple, swapped the genuine Ark for the decoy, and hid the real relic in their luggage.

When Menelik’s caravan departed the next day, the Kebra Nagast claims the Ark’s power lifted their wagons and animals off the ground, propelling them toward Ethiopia at supernatural speed. By the time Zadok discovered the counterfeit and Solomon’s army gave frantic chase, the Ethiopians were long gone.

Christianity was still centuries away, so as tradition has it, the relic was first kept on Tana Qirqos, a remote island on Ethiopia’s Lake Tana, where priests guarded it for 800 years. Then around 340 C.E., when King Ezana of the Aksumite Empire converted to Christianity, the Ark was ceremoniously moved to the newly built Church of Our Lady Mary of Zion in Axum, permanently linking the ancient Israelite covenant with Ethiopian Christianity.

Over the centuries, whenever invaders threatened Axum—including Queen Gudit’s forces in the 10th century and the devastating Adal War in the 16th century—monks spirited the Ark away to mountain strongholds and island refuges until the cathedral could be rebuilt. In the 1960s, Empress Menen Asfaw, wife of Emperor Haile Selassie, funded the dedicated Chapel of the Tablet next door. According to local monastic lore, the move was necessary because a divine “heat” radiating from the Ark was cracking the ancient stone walls of its previous sanctum.

Because the Ethiopian Orthodox Church forbids anyone—including scholars, archaeologists, and even the church’s own patriarch—from laying eyes on the relic, its authenticity remains impossible to verify scientifically. Only one outsider in modern history has claimed to get a look. In 1941, British army officer Edward Ullendorff—later a prominent professor of Ethiopian studies—allegedly used his military clout and fluency in classical Ge’ez to talk his way inside the sanctuary. In a 1992 interview, Ullendorff claimed the chest he saw was merely a “middle- to late-medieval construction.”

However, Ethiopian officials and historians quickly pointed out a flaw in Ullendorff’s scoop: every church in Ethiopia keeps a replica of the Ark, known as a tabot, for ceremonial use. If Ullendorff saw a medieval wooden box, believers argue, he was simply fooled by a decoy—the exact same trick Azariah used on King Solomon 3,000 years ago.

And so, while treasure hunters continue to scour subterranean tunnels in Jerusalem and desert caves across the Middle East, millions of Ethiopians believe the search is already over. The world’s most famous lost treasure isn’t buried in a secret vault—it’s sitting right on a street corner in Axum, hiding in plain sight.

Data Center Backers Suffer Near-Total Midterm Defeats Across the Country

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

More than 40 voting initiatives yielded almost nothing but resistance to data-center projects.

The 2026 midterm election blue wave, which shifted the balance of power in Congress and across state races toward Democrats, can be seen as a repudiation of AI and the controversial data centers that provide its computing power. Data center proponents have heavily backed President Donald Trump and Republican candidates, underscored by xAI CEO Elon Musk investing hundreds of millions of dollars into Texas midterm campaigns to support the industry.

But in many parts of the country, voters didn’t need to make their voices on data centers heard through candidates. They had direct initiatives on the ballot this week that would decide whether data center projects would move ahead in their districts.

In line with a growing backlash against data centers and AI, voters supported about 40 election-day measures that would restrict the construction of new data centers, with only one result — in Harwood, North Dakota — favoring data center expansion.

(Disclosure: Ziff Davis, CNET’s parent company, in 2025 filed a lawsuit against OpenAI, alleging it infringed Ziff Davis copyrights in training and operating its AI systems.)

As broken down by the website Data Centers on the Ballot, Ohio had the most number of voting measures: 21, all of which were in favor of restrictions.

Maine, Illinois, Kansas, New Jersey, California, Florida, Nevada, North Dakota and Wisconsin were the other states with data center voting.

Six elections were held earlier in 2026 in California, Michigan, New Hampshire and Wisconsin, with all of them finding voters opposed to allowing data center builds to continue without new restrictions.

Not all the proposed restrictions were the same. In some elections, voters decided whether to impose a megawatt cap on data center energy use, while other measures addressed specific zoning issues or whether data center projects should require voter approval. Some proposed an outright ban on new data centers, and six of those were successful.

Polls by groups including Pew Research have found Americans have soured on data centers due to high electricity bills, water use and environmental concerns, among other reasons. It’s also hard to separate concerns about data centers from concerns about AI affecting jobs. AI has also become a divisive enough issue to affect midterm voting.

The election results were so bad for the industry that Politico declared data centers to be “the midterms’ other big loser” next to Republicans. Not only did voters enact additional restrictions on builds across the country, but data centers were mentioned in Democratic victory speeches.

“I do not work for data centers. I do not work for Wall Street. I work for you, I work for Ohio,” Senator-elect Sherrod Brown said in his victory speech Tuesday night.

USA Today reported that two mayors, one in Oklahoma and one in Ohio, were recalled after public backlash over proposed data centers.

The midterms might have longer-term effects for tech companies and data center firms. It’s likely that with Democratic control of Congress, there’ll be more scrutiny.

“Public policy is going to reflect public opinion, and there’s been a very substantial change in public opinion just over the last year,” Darrell West, a senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, told Politico.

Political headwinds from the midterms could lead local officials across the country to gain public favor by delaying or canceling data center projects or putting pressure on the companies behind them to offer more attractive deals to communities. The days of huge tax breaks and incentives for data centers could be coming to a close.

“Now, (communities) are pushing data centers to pay taxes, create their own power plants, pay for their use of electricity and water and contribute to local community resources like education funds,” The New York Times reported.
